In all these *kathās,* stories, we are interested, but we are not interested in *kṛṣṇa-kathā.* This means that I am interested in *kathā,* that is my nature, but it has become perverted on account of being contaminated by this material world. This is our position.  
Therefore Prahlāda Mahārāja said that the mind is full of dirty things. Within the core of heart are so many nasty, dirty things—material contamination. But it can be cleansed. *Ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanaṁ*. Caitanya Mahāprabhu has assured. The whole process of education and advancement of civilization is how to cleanse the dirty mind. Why it is dirty? Lusty desire and greediness. So we should always remember that the mind is both the best friend and the worst enemy. If it remains lusty and greedy, it is our worst enemy, and if it is Kṛṣṇa conscious, it is our best friend.  
We have to control our mind. That is very easy, as Ambarīṣa Mahārāja did. We have to fix it up. This *arcana-vidhi* in the temple is meant for gradually making the mind think of Kṛṣṇa. You’ll come to the temple, you’ll see the Deity, you’ll see Kṛṣṇa, and you’ll think of Kṛṣṇa.  
That is the system. Every man, every woman, used to go to the temple. This is our Vedic civilization—to make the mind Kṛṣṇa conscious, always Kṛṣṇa conscious. If you make your mind fixed up in Kṛṣṇa and think of Kṛṣṇa’s activities, this lusty and greedy mind will be cleansed. Not only be cleansed, if you think of sex, you’ll spit. This is the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

\* \* \*

Fixing our mind on Kṛṣṇa is the prime *sādhana* practice to purify the heart, but we products of Kali-yuga culture are not easily able to constantly remember Kṛṣṇa. The Deity worship, preaching, chanting, cleaning, studying, cooking, administrating, plus all the rules and regulations that we follow, are assistants to the prime practice of focusing our minds on Kṛṣṇa and never allowing it to deviate from remembering Him.

## Pride with Humility

**Prabhupāda**:Prahlāda Mahārāja is feeling proud that “You are so affectionate to Your devotee.” The Lord’s name is *bhakta-vatsala.* He can offer His *bhakta* any position, even more than Himself. Just like Mother Yaśodā. She is *bhakta* and the Lord has given her the position for punishing Him. Mother Yaśodā is threatening Kṛṣṇa that “Kṛṣṇa, You were very naughty. Now I shall bind You.” So Kṛṣṇa, out of being afraid, was crying. Kṛṣṇa has given the position to the *bhakta* that “You chastise Me. I’ll accept it.”  
This is a *bhakta’s* position. This is Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy. He can do that. So Prahlāda Mahārāja is appreciating that “I am worthless. I have no qualification.” That should be the position of the *bhaktas* always. Never we should think that “I have become a big *bhakta.”* No. The author of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* says humbly, “I am lower than the worm in the stool.” Does it mean that he is actually low? No. But he’s feeling like that. An advanced devotee is always so humble and meek. He always thinks of himself as worthless.  
And still, Prahlāda thinks, “The Lord is so kind and favorable. That is His causeless mercy. I am not qualified. I am not worthy of this facility.” This is humility.

\* \* \*

Prahlāda Mahārāja’s pride is that the Lord had chosen him as an object of causeless affection, but because he feels that this is not due to any quality he possesses, his pride and his humility do not conflict.

## Understanding Kṛṣṇa

**Prabhupāda**: You have seen the picture of Lord Viṣṇu? He is very, very much decorated. But you’ll see Kṛṣṇa decorated only with Vṛndāvana flowers. That is Kṛṣṇa’s real form. He does not appear with many hands or many ornaments. No. Simple as a boy. So *yaśodā-nandana braja-jana-rañjana yamunā-tīra* … What is that?  
**Devotees**: *Vana-cārī.*  
**Prabhupāda**: Yes. That is Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is always Yaśodā-nandana. He is always ready to be chastised by Mother Yaśodā, by the *gopīs*, and sometimes by His friends. That is Vṛndāvana Kṛṣṇa. That is real Kṛṣṇa and that is real God. Even Brahmā, although such an exalted person who creates this universe, becomes bewildered seeing Kṛṣṇa as a cowherd boy. When Brahmā understood that “A cowherd boy in Vṛndāvana is said to be the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, my master,” he wanted to examine whether Kṛṣṇa, that boy, was actually his master. He took away His calves and cowherd boys for a second and saw that Kṛṣṇa had expanded Himself into so many cows and calves and cowherd boys. Then he submitted. Similarly, Indra also wanted to examine Kṛṣṇa. So everyone, even Lord Brahmā, Lord Indra, and others—big, big demigods—they become bewildered in understanding Kṛṣṇa.

\* \* \*

Lord Brahmā and Lord Indra tend to view the Supreme as the possessor of unlimited opulence. Although we first need to understand Kṛṣṇa’s opulence, concentrating solely on that aspect of Kṛṣṇa will nullify our ability to bathe in the sweetness of the Lord’s Vṛndāvana pastimes. Kṛṣṇa, the cowherd of Vraja, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, can only be understood by practicing *bhakti* following in the footsteps of the residents of Vṛndāvana. Attempting self-realization by our own intelligence or by mixing devotion with fruitive desires makes us skeptical about Kṛṣṇa in His Vṛndāvana form, just as Lord Brahmā and Lord Indra became skeptical.

## The Result of Hearing and Chanting

Chanting sixty-four rounds a day and attentively hearing from Prabhupāda every morning for almost two weeks acted as a miraculous balm on my consciousness. The fetters of other desires began to loosen as my desire to personally serve Prabhupāda increased.

With my *japa* beads in hand, I chanted my way from my small tent, through the fields of chickpeas, to the Māyāpur *gośā1ā*. There I saw my old, eccentric friend, Bhaktījana, who had joined at 26 Second Avenue and whom I had met at our Buffalo temple just before Prabhupāda gave me initiation. We exchanged some pleasantries and he informed me that the temple’s assistant treasurer had just been caught stealing. His *śikhā* had been cut off and he was driven from the temple with sticks.

Bhaktījana was tall and thin, with a long neck. Somehow his hair always looked about the same length, not long and not shaved, but always short and fuzzy. It gave him an otherworldly, impish appearance. But he had a penetrating intelligence.

As Bhaktījana brushed a cow, he explained how he had read in some obscure scientific magazine that flies were actually antiseptic and caused no disease. “I always guessed they didn’t,” agreed Bhaktījana, “because they’re everywhere.”

“Especially in this *gośā1ā*,” I added, swatting several from between us. I decided to share with Bhaktījana the enthusiasm I was feeling in my Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Bhūrijana: You know, I’ve been chanting lots of rounds since I arrived. On Ekādaśī I even got a hundred and eight done. And I’ve been really listening to Prabhupāda’s lectures in the mornings and feeling more and more like increasing my service to him. What do you think I should do?  
**Bhaktījana**: Why not go and tell Prabhupāda?

Somehow or other, I had never considered directly revealing my mind to Prabhupāda. After all, Prabhupāda did have thousands of disciples and an increasingly large movement to oversee. The general mood of his disciples was to not directly approach him, but I was already more or less directly under Prabhupāda’s personal care. Bhaktijana’s advice struck my heart and a light went on. “Yes,” I thought, “I’ll go and tell Prabhupāda.” I immediately headed back across the fields of chickpeas towards Mayapur Chandrodaya Mandir.

I paused in front of the barricade outside of the corridor on the first floor. To the right were Prabhupāda’s rooms. A guard made sure no intruders disturbed Prabhupāda, and Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami stood nearby. Black clouds were filling the sky and the atmosphere was becoming oppressive. A storm seemed imminent. Thunder and lightning suddenly began to lash the sky as the winds increased and the lights went out. I saw a devotee carry a kerosene lantern into Prabhupāda’s room.

I walked over to Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami and told him I wanted to spend a few moments with Prabhupāda as soon as possible. He left to check with Prabhupāda, but returned to inform me that Prabhupāda’s afternoon was already booked with meetings. My meeting was set for eleven o’clock the following morning. Considering the number of devotees at the festival, I was fortunate to receive an appointment for the following morning, but still, within my heart, I wanted to speak to Prabhupāda immediately.

I stood outside the barricade separating me from Prabhupāda’s room. Devotees began scurrying for shelter from the storm. I closed my eyes and opened my heart to Prabhupāda. “O Prabhupāda, please let me serve you. My desire to serve you has increased. Please let me be your servant. Please let my service to you increase.”

Suddenly, I felt a tap on my shoulder. It was Jagadīśa, my old school friend, who had originally visited our Buffalo temple just to talk me out of my involvement in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and who was now a GBC representative. He looked at me with his kindly smile. “This may sound like it’s coming out of the blue, but how would you like to become the president of our temple in Laguna Beach, California?”

I looked at him, amazed. A smile expanded on its own and the ends of my lips felt as if they were nearing my ears. “Out of the blue?” I answered Jagadīśa. “I know which ‘blue’ this is out of.”

“Well, what do you think?” asked Jagadīśa, looking somewhat confused by my answer. I thought for a few moments and answered, “I’m not sure. I don’t have any objection, but I want to do what Prabhupāda wants me to do. I’ll let you know tomorrow after I’ve spoken with Prabhupāda.” Jagadīśa smiled and left me in silence to continue my prayers.

I entered Prabhupāda’s room the next morning promptly at eleven. Prabhupāda was not present, but Tamal Krishna Goswami sat across from Prabhupāda’s desk on a white, linen-covered mattress that spread from one corner of the room to the other. He looked at me and demanded to know the subject of my meeting with Prabhupāda. As I considered suggesting that it was none of his business, I saw that Prabhupāda had entered.

We both offered obeisances and Prabhupāda sat down behind his low desk. He was effulgent, humble, grave, light, elderly, timeless, and deep. By his presence, even the pure atmosphere of Māyāpur-dhāma seemed more rarefied. He gestured that I should speak.

I explained everything: how I had been in Māyāpur for two weeks and how I had increased my chanting to sixty-four rounds a day. I told him how I had been hearing his classes with rapt attention and that by carefully listening to his words, I felt purified and wanted to increase my service to him. I then explained that Jagadīśa had just asked me to become the temple president of the Laguna Beach temple. I told Prabhupāda that it wasn’t that I wanted to become a temple president, but that I wanted to increase my service to him. I finally just asked him what I should do.

**Prabhupāda**: It is my standing order to all my disciples that they should preach. If Kṛṣṇa gives you the opportunity to preach, you should take it. My Guru Mahārāja requested me to preach when I was a young man and it was so many years before I could take it up. Now I am an old man and am preaching, but it is still so nice.

Prabhupāda then leaned back upon his bolster backrest. His right hand moved atop his head and his left hand fell at his side. His face relaxed into a smile for several more seconds before he excused himself and left the room to care for his body. My heart was full, and with my order from Prabhupāda fixed, I offered my obeisances and left the room to find Jagadīśa.

## Only Kṛṣṇa is the Independent Giver of Favors

Prabhupāda continued lecturing each day and I continued hearing attentively.

**Prabhupāda**:

śrī-kṛṣṇa caitanya prabhu doyā koro more  
tomā binā ke doyālu jagat-saṁsāre

Actually we are all hankering after the favor of some big man—or some small man. That is the material world. But we should seek favor from the Supreme Personality of Godhead instead of going here and there.  
That is the point. They have started so many humanitarian institutes to give favor to suffering humanity. But it is not possible. They cannot do it. It is a false attempt. There are so many instances. A father and mother are always ready to show favor to their children. There is no doubt about it. But does it mean that if one has got a very good and rich and able father and mother that therefore the children will be happy? Actually, if the child is destined to suffer, the good father and mother cannot give him shelter. That is not possible.  
Suppose one is condemned by the court to be punished, capital punishment, to be hanged. The father may be a very rich man. If he pleads in the court, “Sir, whatever you want, I’ll give you. Kindly do not condemn my son to be hanged.” Is it that the hanging will be checked? No. Nobody can show another favor against the will of the Supreme. So we can ask favor from anyone, but we must know that nobody can favor us unless it is sanctioned by the Supreme Person. We must always know. It is not that one in a superior position can favor me. No, he cannot show favor unless it is sanctioned by the Supreme. Suppose we approach a rich man to favor us with some contribution or membership fees, but that rich man cannot favor us unless it is sanctioned by Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is there within him and if he says “You give him this money,” the man will give us. Suppose we request somewhere and he does not favor us. We should not be sorry. We should know that Kṛṣṇa did not dictate to him that he should donate. He is not so fortunate, therefore Kṛṣṇa did not dictate as such to him. So there is nothing over which to be sorry. This should be our principle.  
So for anyone to favor another requires some extra endeavor, but Kṛṣṇa does not require anything extra. That is Kṛṣṇa. He can do anything He likes. He does not depend on others. Others depend on Kṛṣṇa’s sanction but Kṛṣṇa does not require anyone’s sanction.

\* \* \*

Faithfully remembering that everything has to be sanctioned by Kṛṣṇa allows a devotee to live free of anxiety. Being dependent on Kṛṣṇa’s sanction allows us to live in His mercy, even in the material world. This key philosophical concept awards us a vision of Kṛṣṇa in every “material” transaction and it keeps a faithful devotee safe in all circumstances.

## Service as an Opportunity

**Prabhupāda**: A devotee should not take up some business which requires very hard endeavor. No. We should take up only simple things. Of course, a devotee does take risks. Just like Hanumān, the servant of Lord Rāmacandra. Lord Rāmacandra wanted information about Sītādevī. So Hanumān did not consider, “How I shall go to the other side of the sea to Laṅkā?” He simply, believing in Lord Rāmacandra, calling “*Jaya* Rāma,” jumped over it.  
Hanumān jumped to Laṅkā, but Rāmacandra Himself had to construct a bridge. Of course, that bridge is also wonderful because the monkeys were bringing stones and throwing the stones into the sea. But the stones were floating. Where is the law of gravitation? Stones floating on the water? It cannot be done by the scientists. But Lord Rāmacandra desired, so the stone became able to float. Otherwise how many stones we shall throw into the sea that it will come to the level of becoming a bridge? But Lord Rāmacandra wanted, “Let it be simplified for the monkeys. So let them bring the stones and they will float. Then we shall go.”  
Without stone He Himself could go, but He wanted some service from the monkeys. There were many other monkeys who were not capable exactly like Hanumān. Therefore they were also given some chance that “You bring some stone. You cannot jump over the sea like Hanumān, so you bring stones, and I will ask the stones to float.”

\* \* \*

We should accept service as an opportunity, whether we feel ourselves capable or not. Ultimately, if Kṛṣṇa desires, our service will be completed. our real success will be our sincere devotional attempts.

## Two More Checks

I went to Prabhupāda’s room and offered him two additional traveler’s checks of $100 each. He smiled and exclaimed, “More checks! Another $200!”

\* \* \*

Tamal Krishna Mahārāja and his Rādhā-Dāmodara Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party had recently offered Prabhupāda a donation of $20,000. Upon receiving that check, Prabhupāda said, “Only $20,000?” As Rāmacandra expected different services from his different monkeys according to their capacity, my tiny donation received a more positive response than Tamal Krishna Goswami’s.

## Gaura-Pūrṇimā, March 15, 1976

**Prabhupāda**: Yudhiṣṭhira Mahārāja was asked by Dharmarāja, “What is the most wonderful thing in this world?” He answered, “The most wonderful thing is that every moment people are dying. On every planet. Not that only on this planet there is death and that in other planets there is no death. No. Within this material world, on every planet, either Brahmā or the small insignificant ant, everyone must die. Everyone has to die. There is no excuse. Yet they are still planning a permanent settlement.  
So Prahlāda Mahārāja and every Vaiṣṇava who is actually following the *paramparā* system of Vaiṣṇava duty, they are anxious. Prahlāda Mahārāja says they are *vimūḍha*. Why? Because, “They don’t like You. They don’t care for You: ‘I don’t care for God. I don’t accept Kṛṣṇa.’” So all the Vaiṣṇavas, especially those who have taken to the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, have this mood. Prahlāda Mahārāja will speak later on that, “Sir, I am not inclined at all to go alone to Vaikuṇṭha. I must take them all with me. Otherwise I don’t want to go.”  
Prahlāda Mahārāja is devotee. He can ask from Kṛṣṇa, “Give me such power that along with me I shall take all of them.” That is a Vaiṣṇava. He can do so.  
Therefore we have to become the dog of a Vaiṣṇava. *Chāḍiyā vaiṣṇava-sevā nistāra pāyeche kebā.* Without serving a Vaiṣṇava, nobody can be delivered. Why? Because a Vaiṣṇava is so merciful. He can demand from Kṛṣṇa, “Please, on my sake, please excuse him. I’ll take him with me.” So Kṛṣṇa grants him, “All right, you take him.”  
A Vaiṣṇava’s only business is how to deliver fallen souls. Caitanya Mahāprabhu appeared as a Vaiṣṇava. He was born in a very nice *brāhmaṇa* family. He had a very beautiful body. Everyone liked Him. By His command He could in one night gather hundreds of thousands of people to make civil disobedience. He was so popular! He was only twenty or twenty-one years old. He had His beautiful wife, a very affectionate mother, and a very good position in the society. Still, He gave up everything.  
Why? “If I do not deliver these fallen souls entrapped in *māyā,* then who will do it?” So for this purpose, Kṛṣṇa comes. For this purpose, Caitanya Mahāprabhu comes. And this is the same purpose of Prahlāda Mahārāja.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda expected his disciples to develop true compassion. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura spoke to the members of the Gaudiya Math before he departed from this world in a similar mood: “I wish that every selfless, tender-hearted person of Gaudiya Math will be prepared to shed two hundred gallons of blood for the nourishment of the spiritual corpus of every individual of this world.”

## A Special Gift Withdrawn

Most devotees left for Vṛndāvana after Gaura-Pūrṇimā. Prabhupāda would be going there in a few days and they would all meet him again. But Prabhupāda continued lecturing in Māyāpur, so I stayed on to hear him speak. I decided that as soon as Prabhupāda departed for Vṛndāvana, I would leave for Australia to prepare for my family’s travel to the United States.

Before leaving Māyāpur, and in Calcutta as well, I continued to listen to Prabhupāda, but I felt my ability to hear attentively waning, as if I had been granted a special gift for a purpose that was now fulfilled.

## Guaranteed Success

**Prabhupāda**: There is regular propaganda, “Why should I go to the temple? God is everywhere.” You can answer, “God is everywhere—except in the temple?” Their propaganda is, “God is everywhere. Why do you go to the temple?” But our answer is, “If God is everywhere, why He is not *also* in the temple? He must also be in the temple.” But these rascals make propaganda, “Don’t go to the temple.”  
There are so many rascals. But they came and they are gone. In India there is propaganda, “There is no God in the temple.” But by this false propaganda the temples have not closed. Still in Vṛndāvana you’ll find five thousand temples. You see the Jagannātha Purī temple. Many thousands of people go there. There is also Raṅganātha temple and so many other temples. So in India, this propaganda will not stay. These rascals will come and go. They make propaganda and go away. But the temples remain and so does the worship. Now the temples are increasing all over the world. Instead of stopping people from going to the temple, the temples are increasing.  
For centuries, there was no temple in Europe and America. Now there are hundreds and thousands of temples. So it will increase. No one can stop Kṛṣṇa’s glories. You cannot stop Kṛṣṇa’s supremacy. That is not possible. *Mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat kiñcid asti dhanañjaya.* That cannot be. Therefore it is sure and certain that if you work very sincerely for Kṛṣṇa to spread His message, the message of *Bhagavad-gītā,* there cannot be any check. It is not possible. *Ahaituky apratihatā. Apratihatā* means it cannot be checked by any material condition. You go on with your duty sincerely. There will be no power to check your progress.

\* \* \*

While in Calcutta, Prabhupāda took his morning walk at the Victoria Memorial. I scooped up dirt from his footprints. Years before, on a similar walk in Calcutta, Prabhupāda had pointed to the Victoria Memorial and reminisced how he had climbed the construction scaffolding during his youth. A devotee noted the height of the memorial and remarked to Prabhupāda, “You must have been very brave.” Prabhupāda replied, “I am still brave. Otherwise, how could I have come alone to your country?”

Prabhupāda was brave and, by his preaching words and preaching deeds, he inspired his disciples to bravely serve Kṛṣṇa. Based on Prabhupāda’s bravery, devotees, filled with strength and encouragement, took great risks and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world.

After weeks in Śrīdhāma Māyāpur hearing Prabhupāda’s potent preaching, I left Calcutta for Australia, inspired to do my part as his servant.

## Melbourne, 1976

Melbourne, 1976

## Traveling Plans via Air-Kṛṣṇa

Prabhupāda’s visit to Melbourne in 1976 found me busy making preparations to close my business. I was soon to leave Australia and become the president of ISKCON Laguna Beach. I arranged to buy inexpensive airplane tickets through the Australian Union of Students, but Kṛṣṇa had another plan. I caught a tram to downtown Melbourne to purchase the tickets at the Union’s office. It was just after noon, but the storefront was locked. A “Closed” sign hung tilted on the door. “That’s strange,” I thought. “It’s a regular business day and there are people inside flurrying around the office. Why is it closed?” I tapped on the glass door, but someone waved me away. I persisted, and finally a young woman pushed the door open a few inches and thrust her head out. “We’re closed,” she said.

“I already figured that out. When’ll you be open again?”

“We won’t,” she said. “The government’s giving us trouble and at noon we closed for good.” She closed the door again. “But one more ticket.” I called as I tapped on the glass, thinking of the huge difference in price between three discounted tickets and three full-fare tickets. She shook her head no and returned to shuffling her papers. As a last “Just one more!” escaped my mouth, it occurred to me that Prabhupāda would be visiting Australia in a few weeks and then on to Hawaii. I could travel to Hawaii with him and then fly to the mainland.

\* \* \*

Up until this point, I hadn’t even considered traveling with Prabhupāda. I was elated by this “minor miracle” of Kṛṣṇa’s closing the ticket office. I immediately phoned the temple, found out Prabhupāda’s onward flight bookings, and made parallel bookings for my family.

## Godbrothers Will Fight

Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami arrived from New Zealand to spend time with Prabhupāda. The local GBC at that time was Guru-kṛpa Swami. As Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami was acting independently of ISKCON, Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa and Guru-kṛpa did not have a cordial relationship.

With both *sannyāsīs*, Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa and Guru-kṛpa, at his feet, Prabhupāda asked them a pointed question:

**Prabhupāda**:What is the cause when two disciples of the same spiritual master cannot get along?  
**Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa**: Envy, Prabhupāda.

\[Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa and Guru-kṛpa both looked at each other\]

**Prabhupāda**: Yes, but who is envious is hard to tell. But time will tell. One branch will blossom and the other will wither.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s ominous statement reveals that an envious mentality, although often difficult to detect, will ultimately cut us off from devotional service and ruin our spiritual lives. Prabhupāda later wrote me about the squabbles and differences of opinion between Godbrothers:

Regarding the other matters, a difference between Godbrothers is natural. That difference of opinion will continue; what can be done. … So it is a natural thing for the brothers to fight, as long as they all stay obedient to the father. You do whatever you like, but do not forget chanting and following the rules and regulations. That will save you.

Fights among Godbrothers should not be taken too seriously; they will always exist. Rather than taking sides, becoming envious of “the opposition,” and committing offenses, we should stick to our actual business of strictly following the rules and regulations of spiritual life.

## The Lord Helps Those Who Help Themselves

A devotee asked Prabhupāda for help, saying that he was not able to follow the four rules. Prabhupāda looked sternly at him, “if you do not follow the regulative principles, then no one can help you.”

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s statement that we must follow the basic regulations before we are able to make advancement is not harsh. Nor is it meant to discourage those who are currently unable to strictly follow.

Sometimes when devotees stop following strictly, they become overtly critical. ISKCON and its leaders may need improvement, but it still seems more appropriate that before attempting to change or revolutionize ISKCON and thus save the world, these devotees should first adhere to Prabhupāda’s basic instruction of following the four rules and chanting sixteen rounds a day.

## Tastes of Sweetness

I had so much to do before our departure for America that I was not able to participate in Prabhupāda’s 1976 Melbourne visit other than to hear his evening *Bhagavad-gītā* classes.

## Innocent or Guilty?

Guests, both known and unknown, filled the temple room. “A study of the *Bhagavad-gītā*,” he said, “could begin from either Chapter One or Chapter Nine.” Prabhupāda chose Chapter Nine. During the class, Prabhupāda said softly, “Don’t ask a *sādhu* a question unless you are prepared to accept his answer.”

He sat proudly after class on the purple velvet *vyāsāsana,* his back straight and his head high. He called for questions. A hand went up and a young man with long hair and glasses stood in the crowded temple room.

**Young Man**:“I’m asking this question … I’m concerned about the perverted caste system in India, right? I’m asking this question about a *brāhmaṇa.* If one of your *brāhmaṇa* initiated disciples falls from this platform, leaves the temple and commits offenses, then returns demanding respect, should he be allowed to keep the sacred thread or should he be engaged in menial service, which never fails to cultivate humility?  
Prabhupāda: Well, that is to be judged by the spiritual master. You cannot judge.  
**Young Man**: Well, I’m asking this because …  
Prabhupāda: You cannot judge who is following, who is not following. That is not your jurisdiction. Therefore he has accepted a spiritual master. It is his jurisdiction. He will do whatever necessary.  
**Young Man**: Has there been a case …  
Prabhupāda: So that case you cannot judge. The case has to be judged by the spiritual master.  
**Young Man**: Could you give me an example of a case that has been …  
Prabhupāda: Just like if somebody has done something wrong, he is taken to the court and the judge gives his decision, whether he is criminal or not. The man who has arrested him, he cannot give the decision. It has to be tried by the higher authority. Then the judgment will be. Anything more?

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda had not been pleased when I left Hong Kong a few years earlier, but when I heard Prabhupāda’s answer, I knew that although he wasn’t pleased, he had judged me innocent.

In general, devotees should refrain from judging each other. That service should be left to the spiritual master.

Here are some other class highlights that struck me:

## The Test of Love

**Prabhupāda**: We are beings. We are individual persons. It is not very difficult to understand. Every one of us, individual. We think individually. We dress individually. We have got our egotism, individual. Everything … I don’t agree with you; you don’t agree with me. Voluntarily sometimes we agree. That means every one of us has individuality. This is called being, “I am.” Similarly, God is also a being like us, but He is the Supreme Being. That is the difference between God and me. I am also a being, you are also a being, but we are not the Supreme Being.

\* \* \*

Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami and other members of the “outside” group of devotees were present. My impression was that Prabhupāda was establishing that disagreements were natural due to our individuality, but we should nevertheless “suspend” this tendency and voluntarily unite around the desires of our spiritual master.

## Freedom from Envy

**Prabhupāda**: *Nirmatsara. Matsarata* means to become envious. So that is one of the qualification to understand God, not to become envious. But our enviousness is beginning from God. As soon as we present Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, so many persons will protest, “Why is Kṛṣṇa the Supreme Personality of Godhead?” Why not? First of all answer why not. If you think Kṛṣṇa is not the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then you present who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Either you do not know or you cannot present. If you do not know who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then take it from me: Kṛṣṇa. And if you know, then you present who is more efficient, more expert, more powerful, more rich than Kṛṣṇa. This is practically a challenge. So actually it has been challenged and it has been scrutinizingly examined and the verdict is *īśvaraḥ paramaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ sac-cid-ānanda-vigrahaḥ, anādir ādir govindaḥ …*  
**Devotees**: … *sarva-kāraṇa-kāraṇam*.  
**Prabhupāda**: This has been decided. *Nṛpa nirṇitaṁ.* It is already considered and decided.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s mode of argument was strong and he would, at his discretion, apply the pressure of his arguments to individuals or groups during class.

## Real Education in Real Knowledge

**Prabhupāda**: There is no education actually. The modern education means simply craftsmanship. if you can prepare a nice motor car, oh, what is that? It is craftsmanship—the blacksmith’s work. It is not knowledge. Knowledge is different. Therefore it is called *jñānaṁ vijñāna-sahitaṁ.* This is knowledge, “What I am? I am this body or something else? Why I am suffering? If there is any remedy? I do not wish to die, neither I am subjected to death.” *Nityaḥ śāśvato ’yam … na hanyate han …* This is knowledge, that “If I am eternal, if I do not die after annihilation of this body, then why I am subjected to this body?” This is knowledge. And to manufacture a motor car, that is not knowledge. That is craftsmanship. Knowledge is here, that “I am eternal. Why I am put into this condition of temporary body, not only one kind of body, but there are 8,400,000 different forms of body, and I have to accept one of them, *tathā dehāntara-prāptir,* according to my *karma.* This is my position. How to get out of it, to inquire about it?” That is knowledge.

\* \* \*

Education in actual knowledge is absent in contemporary civilization, even among the world’s intelligentsia, and Prabhupāda often pointed this out, as he did to Professor Kotovsky in Moscow:

Prabhupāda: No, I’m not talking of religion, belief.  
Prof. Kotovsky: Yes. Yes.  
Prabhupāda: I’m talking of science and philosophy.  
Prof. Kotovsky: Yes, yes … So science and philosophy.  
Prabhupāda: One religion may accept. That is not our purpose. We are talking on the point that if the owner of the body is permanent in spite of different change of the body, then what is the difficulty to understand that when this body will be changed, the owner of the body will have another body?  
Prof. Kotovsky: But another approach is that there is no two phenomena, owner of the body and body.  
Prabhupāda: Yes.  
Prof. Kotovsky: The body and its owner are the same.  
Prabhupāda: No.  
Prof. Kotovsky: When body dies, the owner also dies.  
Prabhupāda: No.  
Prof. Kotovsky: There is no separate …  
Prabhupāda: Why? Why there is no department of knowledge in university to study this fact scientifically? That is my proposition.  
Prof. Kotovsky: Yes.  
Prabhupāda: So that means they are lacking. It may be as you say, or it may be as I say, but there must be a department of knowledge. Now recently, one cardiologist, a doctor, he has accepted that there is soul, in Montreal and Toronto. I had some correspondence with him. So he is strongly in belief that there is soul. So that is another point of view, but we accept knowledge from authority.

\* \* \*

To rectify the lack of transcendental knowledge within the world, Prabhupāda pushed devotees to educate themselves and others by studying, preaching, and distributing his books.

## Finding the Root Solution

**Prabhupāda**:We are manufacturing so many ways of happy life, this “ism,” that “ism,” this plan, that plan. So many plans. We are seeing so many suggestions, in the television. “This is the problem. “This is the suggestion. This is problem.” Full of new, new problems, new, new suggestions. But because we are lacking this *rāja-vidyā,* the king of knowledge, we are perplexed. But if you know that Kṛṣṇa is the root cause of everything and if you serve Kṛṣṇa, then every problem will be solved immediately.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s determined conviction was that Kṛṣṇa consciousness could solve all problems. As a corollary to that, no problems can be solved outside of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

## Special Children, Special Parents

**Prabhupāda**: We have got so many children among our *gṛhastha* devotees. How fine they are. They’re getting Kṛṣṇa consciousness from the beginning of their life. That means in the past they advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Somehow or other they failed to complete. Now they have got again chance, again in the temple, dancing and chanting from the very beginning of life. So this is the opportunity. … Suppose by *karma,* by material activities, fruitive activities, you become very rich man or very learned man and attain a very good office. But everything will be finished with your death. As soon as the body is finished, all your assets are finished. But if you become a devotee, your body may be finished but the soul is eternal. The soul will carry your assets of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and nature will give you another chance of taking birth in a very rich family or in a Vaiṣṇava family.

\* \* \*

Birth in a Kṛṣṇa conscious family is a great opportunity for a soul to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and end his material existence. Prabhupāda therefore carefully explained the duty of a parent.

**Prabhupāda**: This is real contraceptive method. If you think that “If a child is born, I shall educate him in such a way that this birth will be his last birth. Next he is going back to home, back to Godhead,” then produce a child. Otherwise don’t produce. This is spiritual contraceptive. This is real father and mother. Otherwise a dog is also becoming a father. That kind of father-becoming is not recommended in the Vedic civilization. Now, just become a real father to give the real benefit to the son. Then you become a father.

## Bhagavad-gītā for Children

**Prabhupāda**: The finger is the part and parcel of my body. So what is the duty? As soon as I say, “Finger, please come here,” it comes. “Finger, come here. Do this, do that, pick up.” This is a finger’s business. A finger cannot remain independent. Or if the finger cannot give the service properly, then it is diseased. Similarly, our material condition means we are in diseased condition. We are not giving service to God, we are giving service to the dog. This is our diseased condition. So God is Himself explaining how you should live. That is *Bhagavad-gītā.*

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda was such an expert preacher that he could explain difficult philosophy in such a way that everyone could understand.

## The Foundation is Faith

**Prabhupāda**: So if we accept that “Whatever Kṛṣṇa is saying, it is perfect,” this is called *śraddhadhānāḥ,* faith. Without faith you cannot make any progress. In every action you must have faith. Just like if you go to a barber, you spread your neck and he is with a razor. He can immediately cut your throat. But because you have got faith—“No, this barber is honest. He’ll not cut my throat. He’ll cleanly shave …” So faith is required in every step. Without faith you cannot make one step. Simply you have to find out the person, “He is worthy of me keeping my faith.” Here is Kṛṣṇa. He is worthy of you accepting His instruction. So take *Bhagavad-gītā* very seriously. Then your life is successful.

\* \* \*

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī stated, *“ādau śraddhāḥ,”* first there is faith. Faith in *guru* is faith in Kṛṣṇa. A faithful person thinks that his *guru* is accurately representing Kṛṣṇa and thus Kṛṣṇa will be pleased if I please my *guru*. No one progresses in Kṛṣṇa consciousness without faith.

## Getting Correct Answers

**Young Man**: Śrīla Prabhupāda, how does one surrendering to Kṛṣṇa discriminate between being tactful in his speech, on one hand, and, on the other hand, compromising?  
**Prabhupāda**:To surrender to Kṛṣṇa is the best tactful and if you surrender, He will give you instruction. Then all tactfulness will be there.

teṣāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ  
bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam  
dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ  
yena mām upayānti te

If you become a completely surrendered devotee of Kṛṣṇa—He is within your heart—He will give you the right instruction so that in every sphere of life you’ll be perfect.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda explained the one principle that solves all “How do I know what to do?” problems: be sincere enough to receive Kṛṣṇa’s instructions.

## Initiation Within the Heart

Questioner: Śrīla Prabhupāda, does a disciple first accept his spiritual master within the heart when he takes initiation?  
**Devotee**: What is the question again?  
Questioner: When a disciple takes initiation, does he first take initiation within the heart?  
Prabhupāda: Initiation is the third stage. First stage is—just like you have come. This is called *śraddhā,* a little faith, not full faith. Then you have to increase this faith by association of devotees. That is required. And when the faith is increased, then the question of initiation. Initiation should not be immediately offered. That will be misused. Therefore when we initiate, we inquire from the head of the temple, “How long he is coming? What is his behavior?” If he certifies that “Yes, he is doing nice,” then we initiate. Our initiation is not so cheap, “You come and be initiated.” No, we do not do that. We must test you first of all. Then we initiate.  
**Madhudviṣa**: His question was that can you take initiation by accepting the spiritual master in your heart without actually taking …  
Prabhupāda: This is bogus proposition. It has no meaning. *\[Laughter*.\] It has no meaning. If you think within yourself, “I am eating,” will you be satisfied? You starve and simply think, “I have eaten everything.” *\[Laughter*.\] Is that very practical proposal? You must eat. We don’t say all these bogus propositions. All right. Thank you very much.

\* \* \*

The actual intention of the question was not accurately rephrased by Madhudviṣa. (Prabhupāda himself told me in New Vrindaban in 1969 that he accepted his own spiritual master at their first meeting but did not take initiation until years later.) Yet Prabhupāda’s answer to Madhudviṣa’s question is extremely significant and relevant. Madhudviṣa asked whether a devotee could forego the initiation ceremony if he had already mentally accepted his spiritual master. Prabhupāda’s reply is clear and uncompromising: “This is bogus proposition…. We don’t say all these bogus propositions.”

Someone may mentally accept a *guru*, or may even accept him with his whole heart, but if he avoids actually taking initiation, his acceptance is insufficient. Prabhupāda’s example about eating mentally is brilliant. If initiation can also be complete by our “mentally accepting a *guru*,” the *guru* is not given the choice to accept or not to accept the disciple. Prabhupāda always ascribed importance to the initiation ceremony in which both *guru* and disciple formally accepted the mutual responsibilities of their relationship, and he insisted that a fire sacrifice be performed even after initiations were given through the mail.

## Hear! Hear! Hear!

**Man**: What if you hear in your heart, but don’t act in your life?  
**Prabhupāda**: Hm?  
Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa: What if you hear, but don’t act accordingly in your practical life?  
**Man**: You feel it in your heart …  
**Prabhupāda**: No, hear means you do it in practical life. Otherwise what is the hearing? Then just like one stone, he has got ear. Hearing means it will react. That is hearing. The more you hear, the more it will react. Then you practically will do it. Everything comes by hearing. Hearing is so important. just like even in your ordinary education, you go to the school, college, and hear from your professor. Then you become perfect and take your degree. Similarly, the science of God, you simply hear about it. *Śravaṇaṁ kīrtanam.* Then gradually it will purify. Hearing means purifying the dirty things within the heart. That is hearing. *Ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanaṁ. Śṛṇvatāṁ sva-kathāḥ kṛṣṇaḥ, puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ.* Because it is spiritual, hearing has got a potency to purify you. This is not ordinary hearing. This is spiritual hearing, so it has got a potency. *Śṛṇvatāṁ sva-kathāḥ kṛṣṇaḥ, puṇya-śravaṇa-kīrtanaḥ.*

Anyone who takes part in this process of hearing and chanting, he becomes pious, purified. Just like if you touch fire in some way or other, it will act.

\* \* \*

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Prabhupāda both followed Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s conclusion about the potency of hearing, especially *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

nivṛtta-tarṣair upagīyamānād  
bhavauṣadhāc chrotra-mano-’bhirāmāt  
ka uttamaśloka-guṇānuvādāt  
pumān virajyeta vinā paśughnāt

Glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is performed in the paramparā system; that is, it is conveyed from spiritual master to disciple. Such glorification is relished by those no longer interested in the false, temporary glorification of this cosmic manifestation. Descriptions of the Lord are the right medicine for the conditioned soul undergoing repeated birth and death. Therefore, who will cease hearing such glorification of the Lord except a butcher or one who is killing his own self?

—*Bhāg*. 10.1.4

## Winning a Heart; Saving a Soul

**Girl**: Śrīla Prabhupāda, would you please explain why Kṛṣṇa consciousness hadn’t come to the West until now? Why didn’t it come earlier?  
**Prabhupāda**: Because you were not born. After your birth we have come here to take you back to home, back to Godhead. Now take the opportunity; come with us. We were waiting for your birth.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s answer endeared the questioner to his lotus feet.

## Auckland, New Zealand—1976

Auckland, New Zealand—1976

## Steadiness in Service

My family and I joined Prabhupāda and his entourage, who were flying first to Auckland before continuing on to Latoka, Fiji, and then to Honolulu.

Alone with Prabhupāda in the Melbourne airport, Prabhupāda turned to me and asked, “Why are you leaving? Why have you decided to move?” I was quite surprised. I had been living outside the temple and was not able to serve ISKCON full-time. Now Prabhupāda seemed to be questioning, at the eleventh-hour, my attempt to take up more direct service. “But I asked you in Māyāpur, Prabhupāda. They wanted me to become temple president in Laguna Beach, and I asked you in Māyāpur. You gave me permission to accept the position.”

Prabhupāda’s response paralleled the mood in which he had previously spoken. “Permission may be there, but why did you even want to leave?”

\* \* \*

What pleased Prabhupāda? My analysis is that the essential ingredient in pleasing Prabhupāda was steadiness. When I could not remain in Hong Kong, I nevertheless was able to satisfy him by steadily—week by week—rendering service by sending donations. Prabhupāda’s basic desire for his disciples was that they served steadily. Ultimately steady service elevates one. Steady service will be more pleasing to our spiritual master than glowing, but fleeting service, which shines brightly like a shooting star and then quickly becomes extinguished and disappears.

One devotee’s requirements to continue serving may be different from that of another. Later on this same tour, Prabhupāda wrote a purport (*Bhāg.* 8.2.30) that underscores this point: one should situate himself in a position wherein he can most steadily continue his devotional service and cultivate Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In the fighting between the elephant and the crocodile, the difference was that although the elephant was extremely powerful, he was in a foreign place, in the water. During one thousand years of fighting, he could not get any food, and under the circumstances his bodily strength diminished, and because his bodily strength diminished, his mind also became weak and his senses less powerful. The crocodile, however, being an animal of the water, had no difficulties. He was getting food and was therefore getting mental strength and sensual encouragement. Thus while the elephant became reduced in strength, the crocodile became more and more powerful. Now, from this we may take the lesson that in our fight with *māyā* we should not be in a position in which our strength, enthusiasm and senses will be unable to fight vigorously. Our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has actually declared war against the illusory energy, in which all the living entities are rotting in a false understanding of civilization. The soldiers in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement must always possess physical strength, enthusiasm and sensual power. To keep themselves fit, they must therefore place themselves in a normal condition of life. What constitutes a normal condition will not be the same for everyone, and therefore there are divisions of *varṇāśrama*—*brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya, vaiśya, śūdra, brahmacarya, gṛhastha, vānaprastha* and *sannyāsa.* Especially in this age, Kali-yuga, it is advised that no one take *sannyāsa.*

aśvamedhaṁ gavālambhaṁ  
sannyāsaṁ pala-paitṛkam  
devareṇa sutotpattiṁ  
kalau pañca vivarjayet

—Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa

From this we can understand that in this age the *sannyāsa-āśrama* is forbidden because people are not strong. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu showed us an example in taking *sannyāsa* at the age of twenty-four years, but even Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya advised Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to be extremely careful because He had taken *sannyāsa* at an early age. For preaching we give young boys *sannyāsa,* but actually it is being experienced that they are not fit for *sannyāsa.* There is no harm, however, if one thinks that he is unfit for *sannyāsa;* if he is very much agitated sexually, he should go to the *āśrama* where sex is allowed, namely the *gṛhastha-āśrama.* That one has been found to be very weak in one place does not mean that he should stop fighting the crocodile of *māyā.* One should take shelter of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, as we shall see Gajendra do, and at the same time one can be a *gṛhastha* if he is satisfied with sexual indulgence. There is no need to give up the fight. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu therefore recommended, *sthāne sthitāḥ śruti-gatāṁ tanu-vāṅ-manobhiḥ.* One may stay in whichever *āśrama* is suitable for him; it is not essential that one take *sannyāsa.* If one is sexually agitated, he can enter the *gṛhastha-āśrama.* But one must continue fighting. For one who is not in a transcendental position, to take *sannyāsa* artificially is not a very great credit. If *sannyāsa* is not suitable, one may enter the *gṛhastha-āśrama* and fight *māyā* with great strength. But one should not give up the fighting and go away.

## “Just to Encourage My Disciples … ”

Landing in New Zealand, Prabhupāda was greeted by a roaring *kīrtana* and a press conference.

**Interviewer:** Well, what do you hope to achieve by your short visit here to New Zealand, where you don’t have a very big following at all?  
**Prabhupāda:** Just to encourage my disciples. They’re also trying their best to spread this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, so if I occasionally visit, they become encouraged. That’s why.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda once mentioned that he had actually authored the many articles in his BTG magazine although his different disciples had signed them. Similarly, Prabhupāda was preaching throughout the world through his students. To ensure that the preaching would continue, he traveled constantly, despite his old age, and inspired his disciples to preach, act purely, and strictly follow the process.

## The Potency of the Holy Names

**Interviewer:** Well, I’m sure many New Zealanders would like to know the very reason why your followers chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa chant in the street? What is the reason behind this?  
**Prabhupāda:** Reason is that Hare Kṛṣṇa … Kṛṣṇa is the name of God, Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is absolute, God is absolute. God’s name and God, there is no difference. That is absolute idea. So if you chant Kṛṣṇa’s name, that means you associate with Kṛṣṇa directly, God. And by associating with God, you become purified. You are now thinking, “I am this body,” as the dog is also thinking. We now think, “I belong to America,” “I belong to Australia,” “I belong to New Zealand,” “I … India.” These wrong conceptions will go away by chanting. Then we’ll understand that “I am not this or that; I am spirit soul, part and parcel of God.” Then real knowledge begins, and if we act accordingly, our life becomes successful. So this movement is not simply meant for you or me—it is meant for every intelligent human being.

\* \* \*

By their chanting in public, the devotees both purified themselves and allowed others to benefit from hearing Kṛṣṇa’s names.

## Sanātana Gosvāmī Shaves His Head

After the press conference, the devotees whisked Prabhupāda to their Gribblehurst Road temple. The temple was filled to capacity when Prabhupāda arrived. He greeted Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī, the Deities he had installed in 1972.

Many chanters in the temple had long hair, and Prabhupāda’s arrival address made his desires clear. He told the story of Sanātana Gosvāmī’s meeting in Vārāṇasī with Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. During that meeting, Mahāprabhu told Sanātana to shave off his long hair, beard, and mustache and look like a gentleman instead of a *daraveśa,* a hippie.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda knew about the tension between the two New Zealand groups—the regular temple devotees and the followers of Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami. The temple devotees maintained Prabhupāda’s standard of shaved heads while most of those living outside were not adhering to this practice. In his lecture, Prabhupāda expressed his standard: disciples must keep their heads shaved.

## Paraṁ Vijayate Śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam

Later, Prabhupāda met with the temple leaders. Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami told Prabhupāda why the reporter had inquired about the devotees’ public chanting.

**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** They’re feeling threatened by our *kīrtana* parties.  
**Prabhupāda:** Who?  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** He said that some of the town councils are trying to pass laws against our chanting in the streets.  
**Devotee:** Some of our men are going to meet with the council the day after tomorrow. The businessmen have complained. Some of the businessmen have complained.  
**Prabhupāda:** Not all of them. You take some signatures from other businessmen.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda was always extremely practical.

## “Chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa is Bad” is Good

**Prabhupāda:** So there is agitation against chanting. That is also good. Yes, “Hare Kṛṣṇa is bad.” \[*Laughs.*\] So we want that. Let them chant Hare Kṛṣṇa somehow or other. \[*Devotees laugh.*\] “We don’t want to be bothered by Hare Kṛṣṇa.” That means chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.**Devotee:** Because we have our *kīrtana* party now, we go on *kīrtana,* eighty men. We go two nights a week with eighty men. Huge *kīrtana* with five *mṛdaṅgas* and guitars, and we get huge crowds from  
the whole street.  
**Prabhupāda:** That will make you triumphant. Go on *kīrtana.* That is very nice. *Kīrtana* and book distribution. This is also *kīrtana.* Yes. So do it enthusiastically. Keep yourself pure. Nobody will be able to do any harm to you. Kṛṣṇa will give you protection.  
Because they are sinful and in ignorance, they are suffering. Everyone, once they are educated, they will become peaceful, no disturbance. Then you can very happily chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and your life becomes successful. This is our program. But now, due to sinful activities they are suffering. And they will suffer and continue to suffer. So we want to stop that suffering. These rascals, they do not understand. They think, “Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is disturbing.” A rascal civilization. So let us try our best. What can be done?

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda had full faith in the transcendental effects of hearing and chanting the *mahā-mantra* and distributing transcendental knowledge through his books. He also had full faith that Kṛṣṇa would protect His devotees who were preaching earnestly, and he was determined to help the suffering people despite their ignorant resistance to Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.

## A Word of Caution for Preachers

Prabhupāda, aware of the issues between the two groups, spoke a word of caution to the temple leaders.

**Prabhupāda:**Don’t spoil the movement by manufacturing ideas. Don’t do that. Go on in the standard way, keep yourself pure; then the movement is sure to be successful. But if you want to spoil it \[by changing things\] whimsically, then what can be done? It will be spoiled. If you manufacture whims and disagree and fight amongst yourselves, then it will be another edition of these so-called movements. It will lose the spiritual strength. Always remember this.Now, actually, outsiders are surprised. “What power this Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* has got that it is changing people so quickly?” It has to be admitted. Unless it has got power, how it is changing? So we have to keep that power. Don’t make it an ordinary musical vibration. It is a different thing, spiritual. Although it seems like musical vibration, it is completely spiritual. *Mantrauṣadhi-vaśaḥ.* By *mantra,* even the snakes can be charmed. So *mantra* is not ordinary sound vibration. So we have to keep the *mantra* in potency, potent, by offenseless chanting, by remaining pure. If you pollute the *mantra,* then it will lose its effect.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda again reminded the devotees that the *mahā-mantra,* when chanted purely, had great power in and of itself, regardless of how it was presented. The devotees, therefore, should not alter the process by which the *mantra* is received by polluting it with our own material concepts.

## Displeasure and Disease

The next morning at the temple, just before Prabhupāda left for the airport, he oversaw a fire sacrifice and performed an initiation ceremony. I did not attend, as the temple was crowded with local devotees. When he saw me at the airport, Prabhupāda asked where I had been. His frown expressed his displeasure.

\* \* \*

By attending the initiation ceremony, I would have been able to hear Prabhupāda speak. I didn’t have an appropriate reason for missing this valuable opportunity. Prabhupāda immediately recognized this as a flaw, and indicated his disapproval by his frown.

## By the Mercy of a Vaiṣṇava

Jita Girīśa, a devotee who was in New Zealand when Prabhupāda visited there in 1976, has written an account of Prabhupāda’s New Zealand visit from beginning to end. The chaos, the anxieties, the aspirations of a sincere disciple experiencing Prabhupāda’s visit to a small temple are accurately portrayed. I have included it here in its entirety to fill out details of Prabhupāda’s 1976 visit.

It’s a chilly evening near the end of April; six of us from ISKCON’s Gribblehurst Road temple are in a van speeding to the airport.

As we arrive, Yamala Arjuna gasps, “Oh, Prabhu, look at this. There’s at least fifty of them.” A crowd of North Shore devotees are gathered in the arrival lounge, some in *dhotīs,* some with long hair, many appearing in public as devotees for the first time in a year. Yaśomatīnandana Prabhu, our temple president, starts an uproarious *kīrtana.* I feel out of place—am I on the right team? Prabhupāda suddenly appears, smiling broadly. Guru-kṛpa Swami is wearing an outrageous white suit and a beret. They move quickly to the car, which slowly heads off to the temple.

“Hey, that’s offensive—they’re all overtaking Prabhupāda!” Yaśo snaps, as one by one, all the devotees’ cars overtake Prabhupāda’s and speed off to the temple. “We’d better go too, Yaśo. Nobody back at the temple will know what to do.” At the temple there’s a small crew of young devotees still cleaning and cooking, They haven’t slept for three days. The place is a chaotic mess and they are in complete anxiety—Prabhupāda’s coming! Our car pulls in to the temple ahead of Śrīla Prabhupāda and everyone runs in to make sure everything’s in order. I stay on the nature strip waiting to see Prabhupāda. The North Shore devotees arrive and join me on the footpath. They start playing guitars and singing a melancholy slow tune *“nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya …”*

“Are you guys mad? Prabhupāda’s here!” I think to myself. As Prabhupāda steps out of the car, I bound up and down like a puppy greeting its master at the end of the day. “*Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda!” Śrīla Prabhupāda turns his head toward me and smiles right at me—he likes the enthusiasm.  
We all start crowding around the entrance hall at the bottom of the stairs and Prabhupāda slowly ascends as the *kīrtana* explodes with unrestrained, youthful exuberance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda sits calmly on a small *āsana* behind a desk . He has an air of complete ease. This is striking—he’s taking *prasādam* silently in such a crowded room as though this were a completely normal way to eat—in front of seventy people staring at his every movement. He is regal and doesn’t show even a trace of discomfort.

We watch as Prabhupāda pulls small pieces of *purī* off, deftly wraps them around some *sabjī,* and pops them gracefully into his mouth without touching his lips with his fingers. Finally he leans his head back, opens his mouth wide, and expertly, with perfect poise, tips a stream of water from a silver cup. Swan-like perfect movements. This is not a practiced stage routine. “You are very lucky to live in such a nice country. You have enough of everything—enough land, enough grass, enough cows—everything is freely available. There is no difficulty. Use everything in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Live nicely and peacefully and go back home, back to Godhead. Every living being is gradually evolving in consciousness. Even some rocks are growing—there is life there. Gradually evolving through different species to the human platform. The perfection of this evolution of consciousness is to meet Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana. So, nothing is lacking here in New Zealand. Everything is here. I just have one request. You please don’t concoct anything.”

Prabhupāda finishes speaking. “We brought some sweets and ghee from the cows on our farm, Prabhupāda.” Śrīla Prabhupāda opens the big jar of off-white *rasagullās* and tastes one. “Give one to everyone,” he tells Hari Śauri. I get half a *rasagullā.* “So, you had one?” Prabhupāda asks Hari Śauri. He shakes his head. Softly and lovingly Prabhupāda says, “So, you take one.”

It’s early the next morning and we’ve entered the Domain Park from the Grafton Road entrance and huddled in behind Prabhupāda like a group of ducklings. I’m right up close behind him as he walks past a small pond. “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa.” I chant in a neophyte way, as though the *mantra* were a drug to make me high. Prabhupāda notices this and chuckles. We walk up to two small ponds. One has a Viking statue, a warrior on horseback blowing a trumpet.

“Prabhupāda,” Yaśo queries, “were the Vikings *kṣatriyas*?”

“No,” Prabhupāda answers. *“Kṣatriyas* fought on religious principles. They were noble warriors, religious, not murderers.”

“Then what about the modern soldiers? Do they go to the heavenly planets when they die on the battlefield?”

“No, simply demons fighting demons for economic reasons. How can they go to heaven? They go to hell, both sides.”

Prabhupāda walks onto a large green lawn behind the ponds. His mood is so serious it scares me. If he speaks to me, I’ll freeze. He stands, pointing his cane. “What is the name of this tree?” This is not a botanical knowledge test, it is a *guru*-disciple exchange. Will you speculate; will you be foolish and try to impress? If anyone does, he can be almost certain to be chastised: “You think? You do not know!”

Prabhupāda stands like the commander-in-chief of an army about to launch a major offensive. We move on. The same scene is repeated. Yaśomatīnandana and Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami, the leaders of the two camps, contest for Prabhupāda’s recognition and approval, but not many trees are named. The one thought that is reverberating in my head is, “I don’t deserve to be here. I’m nobody. Prabhupāda is so grave and great.”

His head tilted gently back as he aristocratically surveys the surroundings, Śrīla Prabhupāda and his group walk quietly toward the grand old museum building. “This is a nice building,” Prabhupāda remarks.

“Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We’re going to get it for a temple,” Yaśomatīnandana blurts. Silence follows. “In here, in this glasshouse, there are some lotus flowers,” someone says. “Yes, I saw them last time,” Prabhupāda replies. (“That last time was three or four years ago, Jita,” says Yaśo.) Prabhupāda is not interested to see them again. He remembers. Our walk ends.

Next there will be an initiation. Kālasamvara, Parivādī, Sarvākṣa, Rudracandrī, Annapūrṇā, Advaita, and Gauravāṇī will be initiated.

We’re all sitting in the temple room. Guru-kṛpa Swami is in a bad mood—everything’s disorganized. “If you fall asleep one more time, you’re not getting initiated,” he snarls at Sarvākṣa. I start to put bananas and grains on paper plates for the initiates. “Not you! It should be a *brāhmaṇa,*” he tells me. Soon I’m back in the kitchen. Down the passageway comes Prabhupāda’s melodious, even chant. *“Jayatāṁ suratau paṅgo* … “ Then *“karṣan veṇu-svanair gopīr gopīnāthaḥ śriye* …”

The chanting stops. I can’t miss this—we’ve all been waiting so long to see these devotees get their beads. I put down the pot, slink down the passage, and peer in the door just as Advaita and Gauravāṇī get their beads—what ecstatic names. Gauravāṇī, one who preaches the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. “Jita Girīśa! We need more pots!”

Some time passes before Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami’s strained face pokes in the kitchen window. “Prabhupāda’s sitting, waiting on the *vyāsāsana* and there’s no *guru-pūjā* tray. Get it together, you guys. Stop spacing out!” Somehow I frantically manifest an *ārati* tray from the piles of homogeneously mixed kitchenware and Deity paraphernalia and race into the temple room. Two rows of silent devotees line the sides of the *vyāsāsana* where Prabhupāda sits waiting. “Hand out the flowers,” comes an order. The flowers are all on a heavy marble slab at Prabhupāda’s feet and there’s no basket. Prabhupāda looks expectantly. I pick up the slab and carry it around feeling very silly. The *kīrtana* is starting; Prabhupāda nods his head and smiles, appreciating the sincere loving service of the devotees. Then majestically he gathers a handful of flowers in his right hand and sends a cascade streaming into the crowd of devotees who start dancing as though magically infused with life. It is the loving gesture of an exalted father blessing his tiny inexperienced children.

I’m in the kitchen with Dayāsāra most of the day, cooking for an afternoon feast. As it’s being served out, we’re called to Prabhupāda’s room. “What is the noise downstairs?” Prabhupāda asks. “Oh, that’s the feast, Prabhupāda,” Dayāsāra replies. “Feast? What feast is that?” Prabhupāda is surprised. “Go and get Śrīla Prabhupāda a plate of *prasādam* right now!” barks Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami. “How could we be so stupid to forget Prabhupāda?” laments Dayāsāra.

Later we hear Prabhupāda’s angry voice shouting, “What rascal has done this?” A devotee accidentally locked Prabhupāda in the bathroom. We watch him creep up to the door, unbolt it quickly, and run off.

\* \* \*

The evening *Gītā* class is on. Prabhupāda is wearing his glasses, looking old and scholarly as he sits on a *vyāsāsana* smothered in fruits, cellophane-wrapped presents, boxes of dried fruits, and all kinds of gifts. The guests overflow into the hallway and out the front door. “This is my last chance to be with my *guru,*” I think, as I stand right at the side of the *vyāsāsana.* “I’m not going anywhere. I’ve been in the kitchen for two days, but now I’m not leaving for anything.”

Prabhupāda asks for water and I immediately struggle through the crowd to get it. I bring it back, but I’m too timid to hand it to Prabhupāda so I put it on the *vyāsāsana.*

Ahhh! Someone’s punching me in the back. “Get back in the kitchen and cook, Jita!” More punches, but I stay. Damn the consequences. Question time comes. One arrogant hippie stands with folded hands. “If God exists, then why can’t I see Him?” His question is demanding and rude.

“Yes,” Prabhupāda’s voice booms out angry, “you have turned your back on God. Kṛṣṇa is there, but you have your back to Him. How do you expect to see Him?” Prabhupāda’s reply is so forceful and angry that the man sits down quietly. In the literal sense, his back had been turned to Rādhā-Giridhārī; in a deeper sense, his back had been turned to Śrīla Prabhupāda and his chance of becoming qualified to see Kṛṣṇa.

I’m washing pots at about 11 pm. in the kitchen when I’m jolted by a loud thud. Dayāsāra is stretched out asleep on the floor.

My job the next morning is to make *pañcagavya.* Or is it *pañcāmṛta*? Yaśo stayed up watching a Prabhupāda interview on TV, so he’s not around. Prabhupāda is installing Gaura-Nitāi and big Jagannātha Deities this morning, but as usual, nobody’s in charge and nobody knows what to do. I take the *tilaka* and grind it. Next I get the cow urine and stool, put them in a huge bucket, throw in the sugar syrup, the ghee, and then the *tilaka.* I am stirring when Guru-kṛpa comes into the kitchen to see how things are shaping up. He’s agitated. Nothing is arranged properly. “What’s this?” he says, disgusted. “This is useless. Do it properly.” And he walks out. I’ve spent over an hour grinding all the *tilaka* down and blistered my hands. Will I be able to do it again in time? Prabhupāda had showed great reluctance to install the Deities, agreeing at the last moment. Now will he change his mind again if the arrangements aren’t *pakkā*?

At last my duties are finished and I run in to see Prabhupāda offering the ghee lamp, crowded by devotees and half-hidden in a cloud of smoke from the fire sacrifice. He stands stiff, straight, as a servant entrusted with a confidential service. He is indescribably sober, unspeakably grave. He is serving the infinite Supreme Godhead and it is an inconceivably enormous, loving duty.

I go back to the kitchen after Prabhupāda goes upstairs to pack. His breakfast remnants are there! I grab the tray and make for the garden—after all, one can steal *mahā-mahā*! Kālasamvara spots me and begins to scold me. He’s joined by Sarvākṣa who scolds me too, and then Parivādī who is obviously also outraged. I start walking, but the whole temple’s running after me. I don’t remember getting any remnants.

Prabhupāda is about to leave. I have been thinking I am a competent, older devotee, but I’m a fool who has been mostly ignored by his spiritual master—how can I get his recognition and mercy?

At that moment Hari Śauri pops in. “Can you make sure Prabhupāda’s eating bowls are clean, Jita?” I wash them as if they were fragile, priceless treasures, then race out to the driveway. Two rows of devotees flank the driveway leading to the car parked on the street. How will I be recognized? “Prabhupāda, please acknowledge me as your disciple” is my only thought and it’s driving me mad. My life is useless. One of the ladies hands me a garland. Here’s a chance! But when I look at it I see it has no flowers on the neck area—just bare string. On yesterday’s walk, Prabhupāda had removed a similar garland saying, “ This is very troublesome.” I hide the garland behind my back.

Prabhupāda seems to float down the steps, walking slowly with his cane. His skin is golden, smooth, and his forehead is smothered with orange *candana* paste. He is the epitome of loving, gentle dignity. He stops in front of me, bends his head down. “Put the garland on Prabhupāda,” someone prompts. As I put the garland around his neck, Prabhupāda folds his palms together. I am amazed at what has happened. I cannot comprehend Prabhupāda’s unbelievable humility and kindness. I am so worthless and unworthy.

We leap into the van and speed to the airport. All I can think of is how wretched I am. Prabhupāda’s kindness has cut a gaping gash through my false conception of being “together.” I will never be the same again. *“Gurudev! kṛpā-bindu diyā …* “

Showers of carnations bounce from Prabhupāda’s lotus head, thoughtlessly thrown by enthusiastic devotees, but Prabhupāda doesn’t complain as he walks, slow and stately, to the waiting lounge. He sits on a bench with crowds of disciples seated around his feet. He is serene and blissful, radiating an amazing heart-melting Vṛndāvana-śakti like a transcendental force field. A few *“bhāgyavān jīvas*” pass into this zone as pedestrian traffic by divine arrangement. Their hearts of steel are pierced by this *śakti,* their faces smashed like dry, clay masks revealing smiles that have been covered by a million births. We sit in awe, enveloped by this transcendental bubble as Prabhupāda smiles so lovingly.

Hare Rāma dāsa begins strumming slowly and sings, *“Nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya …* “ in a lingering, emotional eulogy. Prabhupāda is listening very carefully. Behind him, Guru-kṛpa Swami rings his *karatālas* vigorously, attempting to liven the *kīrtana* up, but Prabhupāda stops him—he likes it. He sits appreciating his disciple’s mood in a way only Prabhupāda can. He removes his garland, smiles like a father, and hands it to Hare Rāma. Any misconceptions about the North Shore devotees being un-bona fide disappear from my mind as I witness Prabhupāda’s loving dealings. I stand, palms together, desperately trying to show that now I am sincere. Prabhupāda’s glance is like a razor that cuts through to the soul and it seems to say, “You have not even started. Give your life to Kṛṣṇa. Give everything.” Then, soon after, he goes up the stairs, boards his plane to Fiji and is gone.

Prabhupāda, thank you for your unspoken instructions. Please, somehow—whatever it may take—let me be with you again, even for a second.

## A Gift

An “outside” devotee, Ṛṣabha dāsī, cooked some *barfī* for Prabhupāda and asked me to offer it to him during his flight. After we were safely airborne, I left my economy seat and carried the sweets in their metal container covered in plastic to the first-class section where Prabhupāda sat with his servant. I offered him the sweets on behalf of Ṛṣabha dāsī and he smiled and accepted them.

\* \* \*

Hari Śauri later returned the plate to me. Half the sweets were eaten. We honored and distributed Prabhupāda’s remnants and kept the plate with its plastic cover as *mahā-prasādam.* Ṛṣabha dāsī had spontaneously made good use of an opportunity to cook for Prabhupāda and Prabhupāda accepted her gesture. I also had the good fortune of being the instrument of her serving mood; I benefited by handing Prabhupāda the *barfī* and by eating and distributing his remnants. I got to keep the plate from which Prabhupāda had eaten.

## Knowing Prabhupāda

Going through Fiji Immigration, my daughter, Vrindaban Leela, who was one and a half years old, saw Prabhupāda. She excitedly stretched out her hand and pointed a finger in recognition. “Pra-boo-pad!” Prabhupāda looked at the small child and laughed with mild surprise, “She knows me!”

Prabhupāda was pleased and began patting her on the head and stroking her face for some moments, before she ran shyly back to her mother.

\* \* \*

Vrindaban Leela had seen Prabhupāda’s picture and heard his name since birth. Her first words were “Pra-boo-pad,” and when she got a little older, she would say “Pra-boo-pad” when his picture was placed before her. Now she connected Prabhupāda with his picture and excitedly expressed her discovery with her outstretched finger.

## Fiji, 1976

Fiji, 1976

Upendra, along with a wealthy Fiji-Indian supporter and devotee, Deoji Punja, met Prabhupāda at the Lautoka airport and drove Prabhupāda and his entourage to Deoji Punja’s house. As soon as we arrived, Prabhupāda pointed to me and said, “Lead *kīrtana.*” I sang and my daughter danced. Prabhupāda said to me, “She was a great *yogī* in her past life. Yes, it is so. It is *śāstra.* Without carrying Kṛṣṇa consciousness from the previous life, how can she be dancing in *kīrtana* at such an early age?”

\* \* \*

My voice was not great. I felt Prabhupāda was encouraging me and including me in his entourage by requesting me to lead *kīrtana.*

He told me that my daughter was previously a great *yogī,* and to show that he was not just flattering me, he said that it was stated in *śāstra: śucīnāṁ śrīmatāṁ gehe, yoga-bhraṣṭo* ‘*bhijāyate.*

## Why Not Cook Like They Cook?

Deoji Punja’s wife, as well as the other ladies in their family, prepared tasty, Gujarati-style *prasādam.* Prabhupāda appreciated their delicious cooking and after honoring it one day, commented, “Why the devotees in our movement cannot learn to cook like this?”

\* \* \*

After hearing Prabhupāda compliment Deoji Punja’s wife for her cooking and his implied criticism of ISKCON cooks, my first reaction was to find an ISKCON cook to reprimand. But cooking is part of the Vedic-Indian culture. These Fiji-Gujarati ladies, like most Indian ladies, grew up in their mothers’ kitchens. Cooking with excellence is part of their heritage. It would be difficult for a Westerner, who has only recently learned the art, to cook at the same standard as someone who had begun mastering it in their previous birth.

## Listen to Him

Kevin, a devotee living outside the New Zealand temple, had been inspired by Prabhupāda’s “shave-up” lecture in Auckland and had shaved-up. He then decided to join Prabhupāda’s entourage and accompany him to Fiji. When Kevin approached Prabhupāda in Fiji and told him he wanted to surrender, Prabhupāda pointed to Guru-kṛpa Swami and said, “Then surrender to him.”

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda had thousands of disciples. He could not privately instruct or guide them all. He had to rely on his senior disciples and *sannyāsīs* to help him guide the younger devotees, as a father may rely on older sons to care for younger ones. The senior disciples, therefore, carried a burden of love on behalf of their spiritual father to properly represent Prabhupāda’s interests, mood, and affection.

## Paths to Perfection

Guru-kṛpa Swami sat at Prabhupāda’s feet and asked Prabhupāda a pointed question. “Prabhupāda, what about some devotees who are not interested in book distribution? These devotees don’t really seem to be carrying the mood of their spiritual master. They are not into preaching, but instead get into other projects, like farms.”

Prabhupāda replied firmly, “Do you think that the spiritual master is a dumb stone who cannot instruct different disciples in different ways?”

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s vision for worldwide preaching was huge. Therefore he instructed different disciples to develop different aspects of that vision. Perfection is to surrender to the specific order we have been given. We should not be like the foolish servant who brings his master milk, thinking milk better than water, when his spiritual master has requested water from him. Rather, we should focus on our own order. Kṛṣṇa states in *Bhagavad-gītā:*

śreyān sva-dharmo viguṇaḥ  
para-dharmāt sv-anuṣṭhitāt  
sva-dharme nidhanaṁ śreyaḥ  
para-dharmo bhayāvahaḥ

It is far better to perform one’s own prescribed duties, even though faultily, than another’s duties perfectly. Destruction in the course of performing one’s own duty is better than engaging in another’s duties, for to follow another’s path is dangerous.

—Bg. 3.35

## Householders and Eating and Sleeping

Prabhupāda took his morning walk near the ocean. As we walked along the sidewalk, I saw neither waves nor palm trees, only the one-story factories that dotted the side of the road. Prabhupāda spoke about renunciation:

**Guru-kṛpa:** Sanātana Gosvāmī also depended on Kṛṣṇa to provide him food?  
**Prabhupāda:**Yes, but for the renounced order of life, the order is you must beg, *bhikṣā.* Not much. If I can subsist by taking one *capātī,* I’ll simply ask for one *capātī,* not for two *capātīs.* That is *śāstra.* If you can live without any *capātī,* that is very good. But you can ask as much as you require. Not to eat sumptuously and sleep twenty-four hours, no.  
**Bhūrijana:** And what about householders?  
**Prabhupāda:** Oh, householders? Householder can eat the whole world and sleep.

\* \* \*

We all laughed at Prabhupāda’s sarcastic remark. Prabhupāda was pointing out that we are neither householders nor *sannyāsīs;* we are souls with eternal relationships with Kṛṣṇa. Excessive material entanglement, regardless of our *āśrama* status, simply inhibits developing our love for Kṛṣṇa.

Prabhupāda continued to explain the purpose and limits to the concessions granted to householders: “Because he is householder, he has got the concession. Householders are unable \[to refrain from sex indulgence\]; that is their incapability.”Because I am householder, I have got the facility to have sex as many times and eat as much …” That is not householder. That is *gṛhamedhī.* There are two words: *gṛhamedhī* and *gṛhastha. Gṛhastha* is different from *gṛhamedhī.
