Gṛhastha-āśrama.* Although he’s householder, it is *āśrama,* only for advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. That is *gṛhastha.* But a *gṛhamedhī* cannot do that.”

## Opaque Projections

One evening as Prabhupāda sat in his room, he wanted to show his hosts a letter he had received. Although he obviously could have read the letter aloud, he asked his servant to find an opaque projector so that he could project the letter on the wall. When his servant said he couldn’t find one, Prabhupāda expressed his desire that his entourage carry one with them from then on.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda didn’t hesitate to use technology in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Technology’s ingredients, and the creative intelligence behind technology, “spring from but a spark” of Kṛṣṇa’s splendor. Prabhupāda had no interest in false renunciation.

## A Child’s Offering

Our morning programs in Fiji were simple. We gathered with Prabhupāda and offered *guru-pūjā.* We would then chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and hear his class. One morning, as Prabhupāda sat cross-legged on the plush, mauve couch, my daughter—with Jagattāriṇī’s prodding to “show Prabhupāda your doll”—placed at Prabhupāda’s feet her crudely carved, two-inch wooden doll. Prabhupāda accepted her offering, and smiling, picked up the doll. He held it in his fingers for a few moments and examined it, then handed it back to me.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda had previously shown my daughter affection, but she was shy and unresponsive. Another time he offered her a sweet, but she turned and buried her face in her mother’s *sārī.* When Jagattāriṇī coaxed her to take the sweet from Prabhupāda, Vrindaban Leela began to cry. Prabhupāda motioned her not to disturb the child.

Prabhupāda remained affectionate, even from a distance. This morning at *guru-pūjā,* Prabhupāda received the doll as a loving exchange and handed Vrindaban Leela a burgundy flower. Whether dealing with a small child or an adult, Prabhupāda patiently awaited his chance to bestow his affection and mercy.

## Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa

After a few days, several Indian families joined our morning program. One day, Guru-kṛpa Swami danced and led the singing of *“Jīv Jāgo*” and other *bhajanas* for Prabhupāda’s pleasure. Prabhupāda stopped him in the middle of a song and told him to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa so the guests could also sing. Prabhupāda directed us to be conscious and considerate of others so that we would fan, not douse, their spark of enthusiasm to hear and chant.

## An Artful Operation: Instructions for Householders

My wife and I approached Prabhupāda for guidance. I was eager to serve him and ISKCON more directly. In the ensuing conversation, however, Prabhupāda encouraged both of us, especially my wife, to act dutifully as householders in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

**Prabhupāda:**Live separately. You’re a family man—live separately, earn your livelihood. Where is the problem?  
**Bhūrijana:** That’s not what I want. I want to preach.  
**Prabhupāda:** Eh? You cannot preach. Those who are preaching they are *sannyāsīs.* You cannot preach with the *sannyāsīs* and your wife and child. That’s not possible. A preacher must be free man. You have got responsibility. You’re married. You must maintain your wife and children. You cannot get out of this simple responsibility. … Your child is small. You have to take care. You have to take care of your wife. She has surrendered. On my word she went to marry you. She’s so nice girl. That is unique in the Western countries. She never saw you previously. Simply I told her, “Go and marry that boy.” So she went. So nice. You must take responsibility for your wife and children. There is no need of preaching.  
**Bhūrijana:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** Preaching is meant for the *sannyāsīs.* One who has no encumbrance. But a *gṛhastha* also can—as you were doing in Hong Kong. You were doing nice, husband and wife, but you changed your mind. But if you change your mind, there will always be problems. So you fix up your mind. This is my advice.

\* \* \*

This conversation took place after Prabhupāda had taken his massage but before his *prasādam.* He corrected both Jagattāriṇī and I, and we felt relieved that Prabhupāda had diagnosed the problem. He then had us leave so he could eat. Directly after taking his *prasadām,* he called us back to his room.

\* \* \*

**Prabhupāda:** So what is the solution?  
**Bhūrijana:** Well, I would like to serve someone who is serving you.  
**Prabhupāda:** And you?  
**Jagattāriṇī:** I guess the same, but I’m still very confused about it.  
**Prabhupāda:** What is the confusion? Many devotees are already doing. You can also … So now, what will be your program?  
**Bhūrijana:** We’re going to Hawaii on the same flight as you. And then I’ll go to Los Angeles. Then I was supposed to assume the duties in Laguna Beach.  
**Prabhupāda:** Our temple.  
**Bhūrijana:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** That is good. How about you?  
**Jagattāriṇī:** There’s still things I don’t understand. I hear so many people being critical.  
**Prabhupāda:** Why do you worry so much about others? You are supposed to be a faithful wife to your husband. That’s all. So why you are concerned about them?  
**Jagattāriṇī:** Because devotees are in conflict with those I respect.  
**Prabhupāda:** Respect you may have. That is good. We may respect even the ant. One who has no respect is another thing. But as a faithful wife, your duty is to follow your husband. That is your duty. … Otherwise you will be hampered in your advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is my duty to say that. There is no problem. You are husband and wife and you have to follow the instructions of your spiritual master wherever you live. Either you live in the temple or outside. A *gṛhastha* is supposed to live outside because one has to take care of wife and children. But if you remain within our temple, you will become temple president, that’s nice. So manage nicely and keep yourself pure. What is wrong there? Then everything’s all right. We have got arrangement for *gṛhasthas.* We don’t make any distinctions. Simply one has to be in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Study the philosophy and try to distribute this philosophy. Where is the problem? I don’t see the problem.  
\[*Looking at Jagattāriṇī.*\] Your duty is to keep peacefully with your husband, and his duty is to follow strictly the principles given to him by his spiritual master. You have got to live, so live cooperatively. … A wife’s duty is to remain faithful to her husband and if the husband is a pure devotee then both of them will be happy. What is the problem? *\[Referring to an Indian couple.*\] You see they are living happily, husband and wife. The culture is so nice amongst Indians that in any condition the husband and wife live very peacefully.  
**Jagattāriṇī:** I’ve not been brought up in that culture.  
**Prabhupāda:** Ah, but if something is good you should accept.  
**Jagattāriṇī:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** I know in your Western countries the ideas are different, but we have to pick up the best. In Vedic culture, the wife should remain faithful to husband. If the husband is bad then that is another thing. Your husband is not bad; he’s a devotee. So what is wrong there? Don’t disturb your mind. Follow what I have laid down and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Follow the regulative principles and read the books. … You see this girl? \[*Prabhupāda again refers to the Indian couple in whose house we are staying.*\] She is maintaining a temple in the closet. This is Vedic way. It does not require any big hall. Even Kṛṣṇa is agreeable to keep Himself in a closet. I was doing in my childhood also.  
**Jagattāriṇī:** Like this I feel comfortable.  
**Prabhupāda:** Anywhere you can worship. You can keep in a box. It doesn’t mean that you must have a very big temple. No. That is according to means. If you have got money then build temple, just like we have got in Bombay, Vṛndāvana. If money comes, then—otherwise, keep in box. Kṛṣṇa is agreeable. Kṛṣṇa does not say first of all build a big temple, then I will accept your service. He’s agreeable. *Patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalam.* Simply devotion. The real thing is *bhakti.* Either you keep Him in a box or big temple, but if you have got means to build big temple, then don’t keep Him in a box. That is cheating. Kṛṣṇa can understand that too. If you have got no means, He’s agreeable. Keep Him in a box. So devotion to Kṛṣṇa cannot be checked in any circumstances. You can keep yourself anywhere very peacefully. Worship the Deity, small scale, big scale. Where is the problem?  
**Jagattāriṇī:** In a temple, it seems necessary to perform all worship in a complicated fashion and I can’t do that.  
**Prabhupāda:** If you cannot manage big temple, make small temple. But keep it nice. These big temples can be also managed, and so can this temple \[small one\]. So whatever is feasible for you.  
Best thing is to stop taking instruction from anyone else. You take instruction from your husband and if your husband does not give proper instruction, I will give. You can write. I will give.  
**Jagattāriṇī:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** You can take instructions and if you think that he is not competent, then you can ask me. And do not take other instruction. That is not good for a woman.  
**Jagattāriṇī:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** Woman should not divert her attention to anyone else except her husband. That will keep her pure. That is the Vedic culture. A woman’s beauty is how much she’s devoted to her husband. That is beauty, not personal beauty. One who is ugly, his beauty is his knowledge. Similarly, woman’s beauty is how much she’s devoted to her husband. One bird is black just like a crow but her voice is so sweet. The more a woman is devoted to her husband, she’s beautiful. That is Vedic culture.  
Just like Sītā. Rāmacandra’s stepmother served her husband Mahārāja Daśaratha so nicely that Daśaratha Mahārāja wanted to give her some benediction. She said, “I’ll take in time.”  
So when her stepson Rāma was announced to be king, she wanted that her son should be king. So she requested Mahārāja Daśaratha, “You wanted to give me one benediction.” “Yes, you can take.” “So my son should be king and Rāma should be banished, sent to the forest for fourteen years.”  
Mahārāja Daśaratha immediately fainted. But still because he promised, he told Rāma, “This is the position. So, you have to go to the forest, my dear son.” Rāma replied, “Oh yes, father,” and He went. This is ideal. But he did not ask his daughter-in-law Sītā to go. She was also king’s daughter. She was princess. So she could say, “All right, my husband is going to forest. I can go stay with my father’s house.” But instead she said, “No. Rāma, I shall go!” Rāmacandra said, “No! No, you were asked to stay here,” but Sītā replied, “No, that I cannot do. My duty is that where my husband goes, I go.” So she went with her husband and so many chaotic conditions were created simply for her pleasure. And she was kidnapped. Rāma was able to create millions of Sītās, but for one Sītā He fought Rāvaṇa and gave him a lesson in such a way that his whole family was destroyed. Then Sītā was rescued. This is the duty. These *śāstras* have to be learned for duty. Father’s duty, son’s duty, everything ideal. All ideal.  
So first of all, you select, husband and wife, where you shall live very peacefully and live there and follow your husband peacefully. Worship the Deity, individually or collectively, whatever is possible. But don’t be disturbed and create problems. That will spoil your life.  
**Jagattāriṇī:** It seems hard to change one’s mentality when one is not already very submissive.  
**Prabhupāda:** That’s up to you. That requires determination. This I was explaining. Anyone who is seeing woman, he’s attracted. But if he is so determined, “No, I’ll have no more sex life,” then he is safe. Not that because I am thinking of woman I must have. No. I must know, “I am *sannyāsī.* I’ve taken vow before my *guru.* How can I? No.”  
Mind may be disturbed. Mind’s business is to become disturbed. Accept and reject. But I should not be under my mind. That is called *gosvāmī,* not servant of the mind. Mind should be servant, that is all. Otherwise it will be very difficult—*māyā* is very strong. So I must be determined.

\* \* \*

Later, Prabhupāda explained to other disciples, “\[Arjuna\] very intelligently appointed Kṛṣṇa as his spiritual master. Because everyone is in perplexity about the problem of life. So this very thing happened to Arjuna, and he wanted to go to his spiritual master for a solution. And the supreme spiritual master was present before him; therefore he submitted: *śiṣyas te ‘haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam. Kārpaṇya-doṣopahata-svabhāvaḥ … yac chreyaḥ syān niścitaṁ brūhi tan me.* The spiritual master’s duty is how to benefit the disciple, *śreyaḥ.*”

## Two Public Programs

Just after Prabhupāda spoke to Jagattāriṇī and me, he held two public programs. At one, he laid the cornerstone for Lautoka’s Kṛṣṇa-Kāliya temple, and at the other he lectured in Hindi to a full house of locals at a Suva movie theater. Although Prabhupāda had allotted two weeks for his Fiji visit, he decided to travel earlier than expected to ISKCON’s temple in Honolulu, Hawaii.

At the cornerstone-laying ceremony, the devotees held *kīrtana* with *shenāī* through the town to the future site of the Kṛṣṇa-Kāliya temple. Prabhupāda placed the sacred items in the cornerstone hole. He inspired Deoji Punja to take up the responsibility of temple construction. Although Deoji Punja failed to shave his head before the initiation ceremony as Prabhupāda had asked, Prabhupāda nevertheless initiated Deoji Punja and his wife as Vasudeva dāsa and Bhagavatī dāsī.

During the Suva movie theater engagement, Prabhupāda sat on a stage and lectured to a packed house of what appeared to be the working class of the Fiji-Indian community. The master-of-ceremonies, who ran the local Hindu school, brought out several classes of students. The children came on stage and nervously chanted several chapters of the *Bhagavad-gītā.* Prabhupāda watched patiently, fingering his *japa* beads and softly chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

After these engagements, Prabhupāda left for Honolulu. Although he had good facility for his translating work, the preaching opportunities in Fiji were minimal. Prabhupāda seemed to have no desire to relax, or even to spend his days in full-time writing. We, however, were satisfied seeing Prabhupāda every day with only a few devotees present and honoring his *mahā-mahā-prasādam* remnants.

## Light in Darkness

The flight from Fiji to Hawaii was a night flight. The plane was almost empty. I stretched out along three seats when the cabin lights dimmed. I slept well for several hours, but suddenly awoke and sat up. Prabhupāda was directly before my seat and was walking through the sparse yellow light toward the front of the plane. He steadied himself on the armrests as he went. He looked pure and inwardly serene, deep and effulgent like a traveler from a higher dimension. I spontaneously asked, “Prabhupāda, can I help with anything?” His head nodded gently back and forth, and he smiled softly, “No, no need,” and continued on.

\* \* \*

This short vignette impresses a sweet picture upon my consciousness. Prabhupāda, golden in the dim yellow light, walking slowly forward in the airplane cabin—I pray that Kṛṣṇa allows this moment to always remain with me.

## Honolulu, Hawaii—1976

Honolulu, Hawaii—1976

## Beneath a Banyan Tree

The ISKCON temple in Honolulu was donated by Ambarīṣa dāsa, Alfred Ford. In an exclusive section of the city, this stately house-turned-temple included a sprawling banyan tree in the large backyard. If it had been India, that tree would have sheltered either holy men, a temple, or a tea stall. Prabhupāda had previously spoken to the devotees about constructing a new temple building under the tree, but when he heard that we could not provide our own construction labor and that hiring laborers would cost eight dollars an hour per man, he gave up the idea.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda was extremely careful with Kṛṣṇa’s money.

## An End and a Beginning

The frequent and personal exchanges I had with Prabhupāda in Fiji were not possible in the busy Honolulu temple. As soon as we arrived, our association with Prabhupāda was limited to hearing his morning classes about Ajāmila from the Sixth Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* In those classes, Prabhupāda specifically addressed the situation in Hawaii.

## The Enjoying Spirit

The Hawaii devotees were divided into the same two factions as the New Zealand devotees. In addition, Hawaii is permeated by an enjoying spirit. These tiny islands, advertised as the land of fun, sun, and surf, teem with tourists every year who bake on the white beaches and enjoy the night life. This spirit is so strong that even ISKCON Hawaii’s history is littered with mishaps of missing money, drugs, and sex.

Prabhupāda lectured here, in *māyā’s* kingdom, that “Kṛṣṇa consciousness and sense gratification go ill together.”

**Prabhupāda:** This is the material world. So long as one will be engaged in sense enjoyment in different varieties, he will have to remain within this material world. Kṛṣṇa is so kind. We want to enjoy this material world; therefore He has given us full chance: “Yes, you enjoy.”  
But it should be stopped. Yes. Really it has to be stopped. If you want real life of eternal enjoyment, then you have to stop. If you don’t stop, then you remain here. *Bhūtvā bhūtvā pralīyate.* You have to take birth according to your desire, either as Brahmā or as ant, as a cat, as a dog, as demigod, and according to your capacity, Kṛṣṇa will give you: “All right.” *Ye yathā māṁ prapadyante tāṁs tathaiva bhajāmy aham.* If you want from Kṛṣṇa sense enjoyment, He will give you all facilities.  
But Kṛṣṇa does not want. Kṛṣṇa says, *sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja.* That is His mission, that “You will never be happy in this process of sense enjoyment.”

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda once commented that enjoying in the material world was not possible, but if it was possible anywhere, it was possible in Hawaii. These Honolulu lectures were delivered as a counterattack on the philosophy that a devotee could simultaneously enjoy sense gratification and make spiritual advancement.

Surfing is a regular part of Hawaii’s “sun and fun” culture and even temple devotees regularly visited the beaches. Prabhupāda often teased their enjoying spirit. Although this appeared to be an innocent pleasure, Prabhupāda acknowledged its dangers and took exception.

**Prabhupāda:** This morning we were talking about the sufferers, what is called? The sea suffers?  
**Devotees:** Surfers.  
**Prabhupāda:** Surfer, yes. \[*Devotees laugh.*\] Surfer. I call “sufferer.” “Sea-sufferer.” \[*Laughter.*\] We are creating a situation by which we shall become a fish. Yes. Contamination. Just like if you purposely contaminate some disease you must suffer from that disease. Why there are different varieties of life? What is the reason? The reason is that we are infecting. Nature’s law is so perfect that you infect something, some disease, some contamination, then you must suffer. This is nature’s law automatically going on.

So the person who is taking very much pleasure, dancing in the sea like fish, so he is contaminating so that in his next life he will become a fish. He’ll be very free to dance with the ocean. \[*Laughter.*\] Now it will take again millions of years to come to the stage of human being. *Jalajā nava-lakṣāṇi sthāvarā lakṣa-viṁśati.* He has to pass through 900,000 different species of fish life. Then again come to the land. You become trees, plants, and so on. Two million different forms he will have to go through.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda parodied the similarity of the words “surfer” and “sufferer” to point out the danger of becoming contaminated by the desire to enjoy in the water. He did not refrain from compassionately directing the”surfer-sufferers” toward real happiness.

**Prabhupāda:** Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* “All living entities are My parts and parcels.” This means “as good as I am.” But they are concocting, manufacturing ideas to become happy.  
He has no information how to become happy. Actually there is struggle for existence, but they are trying to be happy by manufacturing different types of struggles for existence. The other day someone was saying that they have now discovered another way of happiness. Flying in the air? Yes. Now the sufferers, or what is called?  
**Devotee:** Surfers.  
**Prabhupāda:** Now they want to fly. Another discovery. This is going on. Actually they are manufacturing different ways of suffering. There is no happiness—simply manufacturing different ways of suffering. That is our material disease.  
But here it is recommended that we come to the right conclusion on how we shall become happy before our next death. Then we can end this process of continuously dying and again taking birth and again disease and again old age. That is not very good.  
But they are so fools and rascals, they do not know how life is going on. They cannot see how nature is working upon us. Everything is darkness. Therefore, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the only hope for human society. The only hope. It is not story; it is fact. So those who have come to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I request that you take it very seriously. Read the books and make progress and simply try to understand Kṛṣṇa. Then everything, all problems, will be solved.

\* \* \*

The problems of material life, and also the problems of spiritual life—even the factions among the devotees, inside versus outside—could be solved by śāstric knowledge and Kṛṣṇa conscious advancement. How do we know if we are advancing?

**Prabhupāda:** If one is trained up as a devotee of Kṛṣṇa, then what happens? Very soon one becomes detached. *Vairāgya. Bhakti-yoga* means *vairāgya-vidyā,* how to become detached. That is the test.  
“Whether I am making progress in the *bhakti-yoga*?” The test is within myself. I haven’t got to take a certificate from others. How much I am becoming detached from this material world, that is the test.  
If you are still attached to material pleasures, that means you are not making progress in *bhakti-yoga.* It is a test.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda knew well the tendency of the conditioned soul to shrug off others’ criticisms. He therefore prescribed a self-test for discerning our advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness: we should honestly examine our own position. Only we will be the losers if we delude ourselves into thinking that we can mix sense gratification with Kṛṣṇa consciousness and still make spiritual advancement.

## Pure Enough to Dance with Kṛṣṇa

**Prabhupāda:** Therefore it is said that *suśīlāḥ sādhavo yatra.* In the first word, *suśīlāḥ*—*śīlāḥ* means character and *suśīlāḥ* means very nice character. A devotee cannot be bad in character. Because Kṛṣṇa is *paraṁ brahma paraṁ dhāma pavitraṁ—pavitra* means pure—so impurity cannot approach Him. If one wants to enter fire one must be fire, and if one is not fire, then he will burn.  
If you want happiness, then you have to dance with Kṛṣṇa. But you cannot dance with Kṛṣṇa if you are not pure. Kṛṣṇa is the supreme pure. If you want to take pleasure in the company of Kṛṣṇa and dance in His *rāsa* dance, then you have to become purified. That is *sarvopādhi-vinirmuktaṁ,* freedom from all designation. So this verse is the substance of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement: that those who are conducting this movement, they must be *suśīlāḥ,* very well-behaved. So nobody can find out any fault. That is *suśīlāḥ,* well-behaved. The behavior should be so nice. That is the test of how you have become Kṛṣṇa conscious. That is the test. Spotless. Just study Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s behavior and His character. He is ideal. Throughout His whole life you’ll not find a spot.

\* \* \*

To make a diamond from coal requires pressure. Prabhupāda was encouraging all sincere devotees to apply pressure on themselves and advance.

## The Essence of Material Life

**Prabhupāda:** Every child should be trained up as *brahmacārī.* Then, when he is completely trained up, if he likes he can enter married life, which is a concession for sex life. But it is not required. You’ll find in Vedic culture, therefore, many *naiṣṭika brahmacārīs* who had never any connection with woman.  
And also *upakurvāṇa brahmacārīs. Upakurvāṇa brahmacārī* means he is married but not for enjoying. He is married to beget nice children under the order of his spiritual master. He is also *brahmacārī.* If a *gṛhastha* abides by the order of a *guru* he is also *brahmacārī.*  
So here it is said: *“jitendriya.*” *“Suśila mita-bhuk dakṣaḥ śraddhadhāno jitendriyaḥ.*” The senses should not be used extravagantly. The modern civilization is such that if you can use your senses more and more, then you are civilized. Then you are enjoyer. Vedic civilization is different. The whole scheme is controlling the senses, especially sex, because if we become too much addicted to sex life, then our life is spoiled.

\* \* \*

Unrestricted sex spoils one’s life. Let Freud turn over in his grave and let the modern trend-setters stand in indignant protest. Prabhupāda exposed them all as imperfect and as exploiters. Both *śāstra* and experience prove Prabhupāda correct: our contemporary, hyped-up civilization has brought neither personal nor collective peace or happiness.

We are victims of this modern culture. Can we possibly avoid the chains of sex life? Prabhupāda’s no-compromise answer was as clear in Honolulu as it was in Vṛndāvana: “Yes. By *tapasya.*”

## The Tapasya of Avoiding Sex Life

**Prabhupāda:** Performing *tapasya* is difficult, but the recommendation is *tapasā brahmacaryeṇa.* So from *brahmacarya, tapasya* begins—celibacy, no sex life. If one becomes agitated by seeing a beautiful girl, or for a woman, a beautiful boy, that is natural. Young boy, young girl, they are naturally attracted. It is not surprising. But *tapasā* means that “I have taken vow, no illicit sex. Even if I am attracted, I shall not do this.”  
This is *tapasā.* Not “Because I am now attracted, now I shall enjoy.” That is not *tapasya. Tapasya* means that even if one is attracted, he should not act. That is *tapasya.* It is not very difficult, but one has to practice determination. “I have taken a vow before the Deity at the time of initiation, before the fire, before the spiritual master, before the Vaiṣṇava, that 'I’ll not have illicit sex.’ That is promised. How can I break it?” This is *tapasya.* “I have taken vow before the Deity, before fire, before my spiritual master, before the Vaiṣṇavas, 'no illicit sex, no meat-eating, no drinking or intoxication, no gambling.’ I have promised. If I am gentleman, how can I break my promise?”

\* \* \*

And what realization fixes us, despite the disturbance of material desires, in this *tapasya* of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? Prabhupāda’s answer: practice—based on knowledge.

## Dhīra and Adhīra

**Prabhupāda:** To be agitated in mind is not unnatural. But by practice, then you’ll not be agitated anymore. That is called *dhīra.* You have to become *dhīra. Dhīra* and *adhīra. Dhīra* is sober. Even there is cause of agitation, still he remains firm. And *adhīra* means as soon as there is cause of agitation, he becomes a victim. That is called *adhīra.*  
In lower animal life I have enjoyed the senses in so many ways. So sense enjoyment is not very difficult. Even the hogs and pigs have got facility for sense enjoyment. They do not care even who is who. Even she is mother or she is sister or she is daughter, they will enjoy sex. That is hog life. There is no discrimination. And the monkeys, they are enjoying sex life. So everyone enjoys sex life. “So why should I, who have got this valuable, human form of life, act as hogs and dogs?” Thinking like this is called *tapasya.*

\* \* \*

Actual knowledge impels us to perform *tapasya,* the voluntary acceptance of hardship for advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

## Becoming Safe from Māyā

**Prabhupāda:** So our main problem is sex life, because sex life is the basic principle of material life. Either you are human being, a demigod, a bird, a beast, a fly, a fish, a tree, a plant—in every species the basic principle is sex.  
You have got books. You study and follow the practices. Be a little sober. You’ll become able and Kṛṣṇa will help. As soon as you are very eager, then Kṛṣṇa will help. Therefore the Deity worship is there. Along with Deity worship, we should always pray, “Kṛṣṇa, kindly save me from the pitfalls of *māyā.*” He’ll do it. But if you want to cheat Kṛṣṇa and cheat *guru,* then you’ll be cheated. That’s all. The *guru* will not be cheated. Neither will Kṛṣṇa be cheated. You’ll be cheated.  
That’s all. If you want to be cheated, then do whatever you like and prolong your term of repetition of birth and death. And if you want to stop it, then here: *tapasā brahmacaryeṇa śamena ca damena ca.*

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda again brought the question back to the individual devotee: do you want *māyā* or Kṛṣṇa? He made it clear that those who want to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness must strictly follow the rules and regulations, especially the prohibition against illicit sex.

In this way, Prabhupāda inspired the devotees and warned them of pitfalls on the path. Prabhupāda’s classes at this time also stressed the need to forego our independent plans and surrender to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa.

## Surrender to Guru and Kṛṣṇa

**Prabhupāda:** How to get this perfect knowledge? *Tad viddhi.* First of all you try to learn. How to learn? *Praṇipātena.* By full surrender. Our process of Vedic knowledge is how to surrender, not that I hear and I reject it. That is not the way. That is another rascaldom.  
First of all find out the person where you can surrender. Not that we take a *guru* as a fashion. No. Do not do. Don’t keep an order-supplying *guru* like somebody keeping a dog that the master will order him, “Do this,” and he does. No. It is not fashion. You must approach a *guru* to whom you can surrender. Otherwise don’t take a *guru.* And the *guru* also should not accept such a rascal as a disciple. First of all things is surrender. *Tad viddhi praṇipātena.* The first necessity is you surrender.  
Just like a diseased person goes to the physician and the physician gives him medicine. He takes the medicine and is cured. But he is again afflicted with the disease, and he again goes to the physician. Why does this happen? Because one does not follow the rules and regulations given by the physician.  
Going to a physician means you have to accept some “do not” and some “do.” That is called regulative principle. Without regulative principles you cannot correct yourself. But if simply we say, “Follow the rules and regulations,” it will not stay unless you become a devotee. That is the test of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Because Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so powerful, as soon as you become a devotee, gradually or immediately, all the good qualities come as in your original position.  
So it is recommended that we follow rules and regulations. Unless we do so, there is no possibility of curing our material disease. The whole problem is that we are materially diseased. So if we want to be cured from this disease, we have to follow rules and regulations. Just like a patient, if he wants to be cured from the disease, he has to follow the rules prescribed by the physician.  
“Why shall I surrender?” That is our mentality. If somebody says, “You surrender, I shall give you good instruction,” our answer is, “Oh, why shall I surrender to you?” That is material life. But this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement begins when you are ready to surrender. That is beginning. *Tad viddhi praṇipātena. Praṇipāt* means surrender. *Prakṛṣta-rūpena nipāta.* “Yes, I surrender to you.” That is the beginning. *Bahūnāṁ janmanām ante jñānavān māṁ prapadyate.* To learn the surrender, it will take many, many births. *Bahūnāṁ janmanām ante.*  
But those who are intelligent surrender immediately, thinking, “Kṛṣṇa says, 'Surrender,’ and why not? Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Lord. Let me surrender.” Then his life is successful immediately. Within a second his life is successful. But we are not prepared to surrender. That is our material disease.  
So if we do not surrender to Kṛṣṇa and take His instruction as our life, that is our choice. But if you like, you can make your life successful within a moment simply by surrendering to Kṛṣṇa.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda exposed the tendency to superficially accept a spiritual master yet not follow his instructions. Without following his instructions, Prabhupāda explained, how could we be cured of our coverings of material contamination?

## Meeting for Unity

Prabhupāda met with Siddha-svarūpānanda Goswami, the leader of the outside faction, to try to remove blocks to unity. He first explained the foolish condition of materialistic society.

**Prabhupāda:** Everywhere, living entities, they are taking birth. That’s all. And they are dying also. And between birth and death there is disease and old age. During birth one cannot understand because there is unconscious stage. So even the fetus is killed, it cannot understand because of his fully unconscious stage. But there is life undoubtedly; otherwise how the fetus all of a sudden gets life?  
But these *mūḍhas* cannot see it. In matter there is no life; it will never show any symptom of life. But if there is life, at some time it will come to consciousness. Just like a fainted man is lying like a dead man, but he is not dead. There is life. So because there is life, he may lie down in that unconscious state for two, three hours, but he’ll later again come to consciousness. It is common sense.  
And if a log, wood log, is lying flat, will it come to life? But because these *mūḍhas,* they are taking that “Fetus has no life. Kill it, finish it and eat it.” It is going on. You know that?  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** I imagined that it would soon go on, on a mass scale, that …  
**Prabhupāda:** No, it will go, because they are becoming more *mūḍhas.* There is no intelligence. Therefore I say that we are giving this intelligence. This is the only movement to make these *mūḍhas* intelligent. This is the only movement. Otherwise they are becoming more and more and more and more *mūḍhas.* So under the circumstances, if we also become *mūḍhas,* then who will make the other *mūḍhas* intelligent? That is my point. There are so many things, so many nice instructions.  
There are many other attempts for awakening the human society to right platform, but their basic principle is wrong. They cannot do it. It is not possible. … So this movement should be pushed very vigorously.

\* \* \*

How much compassion Prabhupāda must have felt for the fallen and miserable condition of contemporary society? Society’s basic fault is its dangerous ignorance of spiritual truth. Modern society is so ignorant and degraded that it propagates the most abominable activities, and its degradation is increasing even to the point of eating human fetuses.

How can contemporary society be saved from its horrible course? Prabhupāda wanted ISKCON to save the world. ISKCON must therefore remain free from *mūḍha* tendencies and become fixed in actual knowledge and spiritual truth.

**Prabhupāda:** So far, we have become successful. There will always be enemies as soon as there is something good. That is the way of the material world. Even Kṛṣṇa had enemies, what to speak of us. So many enemies, but He was powerful; He killed them all. Nobody could kill Him, but there were attempts to kill Him right from the very beginning of His birth. He had so many enemies.  
Kaṁsa knew his sister is now newly married, but as soon as there was some foretelling, “Ah, you are taking care of your sister so nicely. The eighth child of this sister will kill you.” He became angry. “Why wait for the eighth child? Kill my sister.”  
Long, long before the birth of Kṛṣṇa, the mother was to be killed. This is the position of this material world. He did not consider, “She is my sister. She is just newly married. Where is pregnancy? Where is child? And that is the eighth child, and what will happen after that?” No consideration. Immediately, “Kill her!” This is the position.  
So we are instructing: no intoxication. So those who are flourishing by selling cigarettes and wine and liquor, they do not like: “Immediately kill him.” Oh, yes, they are thinking, “If this movement becomes very strong, then our business will be lost. Kill him.” So naturally there will be enemies.  
We have forbidden: no illicit sex, no intoxication, no meat-eating, and no gambling. The whole Western world is living on these four pillars. Just see our position. And in the same way, everyone is thinking, “If this movement goes on, then how all these nightclubs will go on? How all breweries will go on? How all slaughterhouses will go on? How the cigarette factories will go on?”  
So you cannot expect that we will have many friends. That is not possible, because the world is full of Kaṁsas, demons. So we have to struggle. In the face of so many obstacles we have come to this standard that there is a Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. It is very dangerous to the modern way of life. They’re feeling the pulse, and everywhere we are meeting obstacles. In Singapore.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** We went to the airport.  
**Prabhupāda:** Yes.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** And practically they didn’t even want to let us stay in the airport. We had to stay there only for four hours to catch the next flight.  
**Prabhupāda:** This is our position. So don’t be disappointed because that means it is successful. Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s movement are not different. Identical. So as Kṛṣṇa was attempted to be killed, many, many years before He appeared … An eighth child, even if the mother produces a child yearly, still it will be eight years before His birth. Yet the mother was attempted to be killed. So there may be attempt like that. And Lord Jesus Christ was killed. So they may kill me also.  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** I don’t think that is possible.  
**Prabhupāda:** No, I mean to say, I am not so important man. But this is the way of the law. If we become weak by factioning, then that is not good. We must be strong.  
Don’t expect that this movement will be accepted …

\* \* \*

Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching confronts, because the prohibitions of Kṛṣṇa consciousness preached purely directly challenge the pillars of materialistic society. A strong and realized preacher is thus a threat to those whose lives, pleasures, and profitable enterprises are based on the sinful practices of Kali-yuga. In ISKCON’s early days, Prabhupāda was pleased to hear that Alan Watts, an atheistic author popular in the ‘60s and ‘70s, became angry when asked to read and review Prabhupāda’s original *Bhagavad-gītā* manuscript. Prabhupāda’s reply: ”Our preaching is proved successful when the demons are disturbed.”

Prabhupāda concluded that we should remain united to defeat modern civilization. Strength grows in unity; weakness is minimized.

**Prabhupāda:** So the other day I have explained that *guru* is accepted as good as The Supreme Personality of Godhead. You have typed it?  
**Devotee:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** Yes, *arcye viṣṇau śilā-dhīr guruṣu nara-matir. Nārakī,* if anyone thinks *arcā-vigraha,* the Deities, as made of stone, made of earth, or made of something material, and *guru,* the spiritual master, “He is an ordinary man”—these thoughts are forbidden.  
So why *guru* is to be considered *sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair,* exactly like the Supreme Personality of Godhead? That reason is given there. That reason is that he is giving Kṛṣṇa-knowledge; therefore he is as good as Kṛṣṇa. Even though his family members or his \[previous\] friends \[may be remembering him as he was and\] thinking, “Oh, he has now become *guru*?” still he should be considered the Supreme Personality of Godhead.  
That reason is also given there, that even Kṛṣṇa was taken as ordinary man. But does it mean that He has become ordinary? Similarly our movement may appear just like other movements, but because the movement is giving Kṛṣṇa, that means it is as good as Kṛṣṇa.  
This is the example. *Ājñāya hañā* \[follow the order\]. It may appear to others that there are so many movements and the hippies have taken another, but it is not that. It is actually Kṛṣṇa.  
So long it adheres to the principle, and not think “I’ll enjoy” or, “I’ll be unaccepted.” Otherwise it will become an ordinary movement. This same man, he’s *guru,* so long he gives the real knowledge of Kṛṣṇa. And the same man, he’s ordinary man, as soon as he cannot give. Same thing, just like a stone doll, when it is worshiped according to the regulative principles—Kṛṣṇa. And the same doll, kept in the sculptor’s showroom, it is stone. So if we keep our movement pure, then you are as strong as Kṛṣṇa. And as soon as you deviate from it, immediately ordinary. This is the secret. Now it is up to us, how to keep it pure. Then no enemy can kill us. Nobody can kill you. That purity is wanted.

\* \* \*

A *guru* is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa because he represents Kṛṣṇa in distributing Kṛṣṇa-knowledge. Similarly, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is also Kṛṣṇa for it too gives Kṛṣṇa-knowledge. Ordinary people may consider a *guru* ordinary, but he is nevertheless extraordinary because he performs his function purely in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Prabhupāda then cautioned about two dangers that can dilute the mission’s potency: becoming impure by thinking, “I’ll enjoy,” and attempting to appease people, thinking “I’ll be unacceptable” if I don’t. His conclusion? “That purity is wanted.”

**Prabhupāda:** So what is the difficulty? Follow the rules and regulations, worship the Deity, and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* as you have been given. Then you will remain as strong as Kṛṣṇa. And if we manufacture some idea also, that is no good. Kṛṣṇa cannot be manufactured; Kṛṣṇa is Kṛṣṇa. You cannot manufacture another form, competitor of Kṛṣṇa. That is no good. Then failure. Just like \[the competitor\] who came even in the lifetime of Kṛṣṇa, Pauṇḍraka.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** With artificial four hands. You know that? \[*Laughs.*\] This movement is actually the real movement to raise the human society. And if we create factions, then the movement will be less strong and will not act.  
So I wish that we should combine the work very vigorously. Just like women. You keep some women together and they fight. Children together and they will fight. Dogs together, they will fight. But sane men, why they will fight? Where is the cause of fight?  
Our cause is common, how to push on. That soberness must be there. I can give you points, instructions, but you will have to put it into shape. Otherwise, how it will go on?  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** We can understand that Your Divine Grace does not want factions.  
**Prabhupāda:** That will better the movement.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda has clearly stated his position: the movement can remain “as strong as Kṛṣṇa” if no false or “competitor” idea or process is manufactured. Factions based on opposing ideas will reduce the movement’s strength and efficacy. Thus Prabhupāda clearly expresses his desire that the devotees “combinedly” and “vigorously” preach the common cause. The urgency of the preaching mission necessitates unity.

**Guru-kṛpa:** Sometimes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it just seems there is a difference in the way Kṛṣṇa consciousness is being …  
**Prabhupāda:** Why different?  
**Guru-kṛpa:** Well, there’s only one way …  
**Prabhupāda:** So now why there should be different? The formula is there. Why there should be difference?  
**Guru-kṛpa:** Then there is someone who’s not doing it properly according to the …  
**Prabhupāda:** That should be settled up. Suppose you are not doing properly. I can say, “This is this,” and show you how to do it another way.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** We should work together.  
**Prabhupāda:** That I want.  
**Hari-śauri:** There’s no problem, but the thing is they simply don’t want to cooperate with the ISKCON movement.  
**Prabhupāda:** But why?  
**Hari-śauri:** That no one knows. They simply just don’t want to mix.  
**Prabhupāda:** That we can settle up by sitting together. Why there should be difference?

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda has expressed his desire for unity. Now the devotees will begin to bring specific issues of contention to Prabhupāda’s attention. Tension between the individual devotees present over methods of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness becomes apparent, yet Prabhupāda nevertheless remains hopeful that they can reach a positive resolution by “sitting together” in his presence.

**Hari-śauri:** Just like in New Zealand. Originally they split from ISKCON and now gradually you are bringing them back to the same standard again, like now you’re encouraging them to build a temple and install Deities. That means they have to come up to the required brahminical standard again. So why was there any need to go away in the first place? But still they won’t cooperate. Now they’ll have to do it their own way. This is … that’s their problem.  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** There are a lot of devotees here who follow the principles but who don’t shave-up. And they still wear *karmī* clothes pretty much. But they’re clean. They’re devotees. And in this way they’re attracting many of the local people, because they’re able to relate to them.  
**Prabhupāda:** So that …  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** They’re not lowering your standard.  
**Prabhupāda:** No, naturally, but when they are initiated they must shave. They must keep to the standard. If one becomes initiated and he still keeps the hippie form, that does not look nice. Do you think that is all right? No, that is not good. So long they are coming as outsider, joining *kīrtana,* they may have their own dress. It doesn’t matter. But when they are to be initiated, they must follow the rules and regulations given by the spiritual master.  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** So …  
**Prabhupāda:** Otherwise they should not be initiated. It is simple thing. Let them go on chanting, taking *prasādam.* But when they are to be initiated, they mustfollow. If you don’t want to disturb them, let them come, chant, dance, take *prasādam.* We have no objection. But don’t recommend them for initiation unless he agrees to the rules and regulations given by the spiritual master. Where is the wrong? Where is the difficulty? You can talk with him like that.  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** I think they only feel that because …  
**Prabhupāda:** No feeling. If you … when you are surrendering to the spiritual … *Śiṣyas te ‘haṁ śādhi māṁ … prapannam.* Find out this verse. *Tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā.*

**Hari-śauri:**

tad viddhi praṇipātena  
paripraśnena sevayā  
upadekṣyanti te jñānam  
jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ

“Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized soul can impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth.”  
**Prabhupāda:** You have to submit. You cannot remain independent. That is the first condition. *Śiṣyas te ‘ham, śiṣya. Śiṣya* means voluntarily accepting the rules offered by the spiritual master. That is *śiṣya:* “Ah yes, I agree to abide by your order.” Then he becomes *śiṣya.* Otherwise, where is the question … “I am thinking like this, I am thinking … “ So long you are thinking otherwise, you don’t try to become a *śiṣya.* You remain outside and you are welcome: chant, dance, take *prasādam,* and remain independent. There is no objection. But when you become *śiṣya,* then you cannot remain independent.  
These things convince him. Then you don’t become *śiṣya.* Remain as friend, there is no harm. Just like so many people, they come. So if these things … This way. What is the wrong there?  
**Hari-śauri:** So then part of initiation means that you’re prepared to do something more than simply chant and eat *prasādam*?  
**Prabhupāda:** Initiation means to follow instructions of the spiritual master. And if there is still hesitation to surrender to the rules and regulations, then all other …  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** I think it is pretty clear, Śrīla Prabhupāda. But just for clarification, they’re working. They have a very nice store, and they’re distributing foodstuffs to all the other stores on the island. And they’re working a lot within the society with the *karmīs,* I guess. So their thinking is that “If we shave our heads and wear robes, like that, then they’ll look at us …  
**Prabhupāda:** Who is prohibiting to chant? You can chant, you can dance, you can take *prasādam.*

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda insists that those chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa who desire to take initiation must come up to the standard of surrender and give up their independence. They must abide by Prabhupāda’s order, his rules, and his regulations. Those not up to that standard may chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and remain independent, as friends. He has seen that many “outside” devotees, both in New Zealand and Hawaii, have long hair. He will not allow that standard for initiated disciples: “If one becomes initiated and he still keeps the hippie form, that does not look nice. Do you think that is all right? No, that is not good.” No great contention seems yet to have arisen.

**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** Can they actually say that “His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is my spiritual master” if they haven’t accepted all of the instructions?  
**Prabhupāda:** Eh?  
**Hari-śauri:** One of the differences that first sprang up between the two groups was that one group was going out when you were saying, “Go out and distribute books profusely,” so they were going and distributing. And they had various means of doing it. Some were not so good, but still the books were going out. Then the group that split away, they were saying, “Ah, you’re disturbing the public by these methods of book distribution. We don’t want …” This is what happened in Australia, the one temple in Brisbane. They became very much against the *saṅkīrtana* methods and as a result they gave up the *saṅkīrtana* program altogether and went to live on farms like this. They left the temple and the temple was finished. So like this, they were saying, “We’re still following Prabhupāda’s instructions, but we …” But they were saying that the devotees who were distributing the books, they were not following instructions. Like that.  
**Prabhupāda:** His Divine Grace has said that “You distribute book,” and why do you say that?  
**Hari-śauri:** Well, their idea was that because sometimes the public is becoming disturbed by the book distribution, therefore it’s not being done correctly. So it should be stopped.  
**Prabhupāda:** Public may be disturbed, but we are following our own course of action. It is not obligatory. We are requesting you, “Take this book.” That is not obligatory. “If you like, you can take. If you don’t like, don’t take.”  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** I think that …  
**Hari-śauri:** So what their idea was that we shall build some public relations, like you said with this store. They prefer to try to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness by public relations, by giving a good impression, and then people will come.  
**Prabhupāda:** Then you are dictated by the public, not by the dictation of your spiritual master. Spiritual master has ordered you to distribute books. You shall do that. That is called obedience. Now the public may take or not take, that is public’s option. But my duty is—because spiritual master has said—I must try my best.  
Spiritual master has not said that “You must sell so many books daily, otherwise I will reject you.” He has not said that. So everyone may try his best. That’s all. The public may take or not take. It doesn’t matter. And if you want to please the public, the public says that “You dance naked, I will be very happy with you.” So I’ll have to do that? Then what is the use of taking a spiritual master? Public, they have got their whims, how to become pleased. So we are to follow all these things? We have to follow our instruction of the spiritual master. Why to manufacture, “The public will be pleased like this?” Public may or may not give you, what you can do?  
**Hari-śauri:** Our success is in the spiritual master’s pleasure, not the public’s pleasure.  
**Prabhupāda:** Yes, that is *bhakti.* Otherwise why Kṛṣṇa says, *sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*? “You haven’t got to please so many religious instructions. You simply please Me.”  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** I don’t think the devotees who factioned out were against book distribution. I think that a little bit was that some of the devotees were using very, very forceful tactics and trying to give people books and take their money, and people were becoming very offended …  
**Prabhupāda:** That is not good.  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** That is not what you want.  
**Hari-śauri:** That’s all right, but don’t give up the book distribution.  
**Siddha-svarūpa:** Yes.  
**Hari-śauri:** This is what happened. The temples fell down, the devotees went away. The ones who were distributing the books went on and distributed more books, became more expert, so that the public was not so much disturbed, and still they’re distributing books. But these people who factioned off are not distributing books even now, two or three years later. So it was simply actually a question of faultfinding more than earnest desire to please the spiritual master.  
**Prabhupāda:** Yes. \[*Pause.*\] Discuss.  
**Devotees:** All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

\* \* \*

Different presentations of individually perceived reality could elicit their own responses from Prabhupāda. Prabhupāda’s reaction to Hari Śauri’s description of being “against the *saṅkīrtana* methods, and as a result they gave up the *saṅkīrtana* program altogether” brought Prabhupāda to comment that we are not dictated by the public but by the attempt to please our spiritual master. Yet Prabhupāda responded to Siddha-svarūpa’s presentation of forceful, inappropriate book distribution and money collection tactics that offended people with, “That is not good.” All points seemed acceptable to both Prabhupāda and the assembled devotees. Amidst the clash of history, opinions, and personalities, the conversation ended with Prabhupāda’s request to “Discuss.” Prabhupāda had expressed his desires and reached his own conclusions. He left the matter somewhat open and requested his disciples to combinedly settle their differences and satisfy their spiritual master.

## A Lost Son Visits Home

Govinda dāsī and Gaurasundara had opened the first temple in Honolulu in 1969. After a few years of struggle, the temple began to flourish. By the end of 1973, however, ISKCON Hawaii fell apart. Gaurasundara abandoned his wife and the regulative principles and sold the temple. Prabhupāda had questioned what had happened to the money from the sale of the building, but Gaurasundara remained aloof from both the movement and Prabhupāda. Although always affectionate, Prabhupāda criticized Gaurasundara heavily.

In 1976, Gaurasundara visited Prabhupāda for the first time in more than two years. I was not in the room during Gaurasundara’s *darśana* with Prabhupāda, but when Gaurasundara came out of Prabhupāda’s room, his heavy brow seemed even heavier. I asked him what Prabhupāda had said. He answered, “Don’t try to become *guru* prematurely. You should serve your *guru.*”

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda had long ago analyzed Gaurasundara’s problem. I remember him mentioning it to me in 1974 when we were together in Hong Kong. Prabhupāda now had the opportunity to give the same analysis directly to Gaurasundara: a premature, possibly lethal, desire to become *guru.*

An actual *guru* always remains the servant of his own *guru* and the predecessor *ācāryas.* Attempting to become a *guru* without one’s own *guru’s* order is a materialistic ambition. Accepting followers and circumventing one’s own *guru* is offensive. Prabhupāda implored Gaurasundara to rectify this serious fault in his Kṛṣṇa consciousness and humbly return to the shelter of his spiritual master. One year later, when Gaurasundara visited Prabhupāda in Vṛndāvana, Prabhupāda conversed with Gaurasundara with great affection, like a lost son who has returned home:

**Prabhupāda:** Why not stay here for some time? So what are your activities now?  
**Gaurasundara:** In Hawaii we have Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities and bathing, Deity worship and reading your books.  
**Prabhupāda:** Very good.  
**Gaurasundara:** Feast program.  
**Prabhupāda:** May Kṛṣṇa help you. How many inmates are there?  
**Gaurasundara:** Right now there’s about one dozen people in the farm, and on Sundays maybe forty, fifty people coming for feast.  
**Prabhupāda:** Very good. You are my old disciple. So I’ll be very pleased if you keep the principles. It doesn’t matter wherever you live and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness wherever you feel happy, and do not forget the real business. …

Because you are maintaining the temple as private. So make nice clothes, decorations. Deity is small Kṛṣṇa or big Kṛṣṇa, the same thing. When Kṛṣṇa was child on the lap of His mother, the same Kṛṣṇa killed Pūtanā. And when He was killing other demons in His young age, the same. Avyaya. He does not change. Acyuta. These are the names of Kṛṣṇa, so it does not make any difference. So you have got small Deity. Very nicely … So you can bathe Them nicely, decorate Them nicely, give Them … And big Deity will be difficult to handle. Better keep this Deity and increase the devotional. More time devoted for taking class, for cleansing … About *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* consider. That is main business.

arcāyām eva haraye  
pūjāṁ yaḥ śraddhayehate  
na tad-bhakteṣu cānyeṣu  
sa bhaktaḥ prākṛtaḥ smṛtaḥ

If you simply worship the Deity in gorgeous way and do not preach, see to the interest of devotees and other common men, then you’ll remain in the *prākṛta* state, material stage. You should go further, see the interest of ordinary people, how they are understanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Tad-bhakteṣu.* To give worship to the *guru* and honor them. Simply to worship Deity is material platform. *Gṛhasthas,* we want, this preaching.

In Hawaii where you are living, that is quite comfortable?

**Gaurasundara:** It’s in the country.  
**Prabhupāda:** Seaside?  
**Gaurasundara:** A few miles up on the side of the mountain. About one thousand feet elevation.  
**Prabhupāda:** All the inmates, they are following rules and regulations?  
**Gaurasundara:** Oh, yes, yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** That’s nice. No intoxication?  
**Gaurasundara:** No, no intoxication.  
**Prabhupāda:** \[chuckles.\] Be intoxicated with Kṛṣṇa.

## Medicine for Uniting the Factions

The devotees in the outside faction lived separately from the temple, preferring to practice their *sādhana* at home. The other faction, the temple devotees, stressed temple life and group living.

Prabhupāda, of course, did not say that devotional service could only be performed while living in the temple, but he did insist that all devotees strictly follow the rules and regulations given by their spiritual master.

Prabhupāda encouraged temple life because the temple routine makes it easy to both follow *sādhana* and associate with devotees. Living in a temple strengthens a devotee’s spiritual practices. Yet, a temple has to be managed. There has to be efficient organization, maintenance of the building, and managerial headaches. Should (or can) an individual devotee avoid this and live alone if he has the spiritual strength to do so? And if not, why not? Prabhupāda’s morning *Bhāgavatam* lectures helped devotees understand what they would have to do to unite the factions in ISKCON Hawaii.

By listening closely to Prabhupāda’s message, I realized that although he was specifically addressing the Hawaiian situation, his words would create a spiritual revolution anywhere they were heard and applied.

## Compassion: the Mood of Vaiṣṇavas

**Prabhupāda:** That is Vaiṣṇava. Parīkṣit Mahārāja is Vaiṣṇava. He is very unhappy that so many living entities are rotting in a hellish condition of life. So he is asking, *adhuneha mahā-bhāga yathaiva narakān naraḥ, nānogra-yātanān neyāt tan me vyākhyātum arhasi* “I am very sorry that so many living entities are suffering. Is there any way to give them relief?”  
That is Vaiṣṇava. One who is not a Vaiṣṇava sees another person is suffering and he doesn’t care: “Let him suffer.” Society has come to that state. I heard from one of my disciples that here, or in New York, if somebody is killed on the road, nobody will care. Is it a fact?  
**Devotee:** Yes.  
**Prabhupāda:** So we have come to that stage of civilization. But Parīkṣit Mahārāja is feeling compassion simply by hearing from Śukadeva Gosvāmī that “A person, if he commits sinful activities, he suffers.” He is not even seeing yet he feels compassion: “How such a person can be delivered?”  
This is the inquiry, that “They are suffering from some sinful activities. How they can be saved?” This is Vaiṣṇava. Vaiṣṇava means *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī.* A Vaiṣṇava is unhappy by seeing others’ unhappiness. Personally he has no unhappiness. Like Nityānanda Prabhu. *Hā hā prabhu nityānanda, premānanda sukhī, kṛpābalokana koro āmi boro duḥkhī. Nitya* means eternally and *ānanda* means happy. So Nityānanda has no unhappiness.  
He is happy personally, but because He knows that “These drunkards, woman-hunters, prostitute-hunters will suffer very, very severely, so why not deliver them?” This is Nityānanda Prabhu. This is a Vaiṣṇava. In your country there is Lord Jesus Christ. When he was being crucified, still he was saying, “My Lord, excuse them. They do not know what they are doing.” This is Vaiṣṇava.  
They are not unhappy themselves and they can tolerate any unhappy position. They come to deliver so many fallen souls. Here Parīkṣit Mahārāja, out of compassion, inquired from Śukadeva Gosvāmī, “These persons are rotting in hell. Is there any means to deliver them?”

\* \* \*

In this lecture, Prabhupāda gave us an insight into his own mood—the mood of a compassionate Vaiṣṇava. His description of Jagāi and Mādhāi as rogues and thieves, and as addicted to intoxication and prostitution, rings true for much of the population in Kali-yuga.

Prabhupāda hoped that we would become compassionate Vaiṣṇavas, but if that was not possible, then we should become the servants of a compassionate Vaiṣṇava. He did not want the devotees to remain aloof from the outside world; he wanted them to mend their differences and cooperate to actively and expertly preach Kṛṣṇa’s glories in *māyā’s* kingdom.

## The Expertise of a Devotee

**Prabhupāda:** Whatever business is entrusted to him, a devotee does very nicely. That is called *dakṣa,* expertise. Just like Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī. He had no interest in material things. Although his father’s estate was very big, he was not interested. But when put in a political situation, he tackled it very nicely. This is *dakṣa.*  
Not that because he has become Kṛṣṇa conscious and a Vaiṣṇava, he is unable to do anything of this material world. No. One who is Kṛṣṇa conscious, he is conscious of everything and he knows how to deal with everything. That is called *dakṣa.* Not that “Because I have become Kṛṣṇa conscious I can have no knowledge in other things.” No. You must have. If not complete knowledge, a devotee knows something of everything. That is intelligence. To know something of everything and to know everything of something. That is wanted. You know everything of devotional service, but a devotee should not be callous in material dealings. He should know something of everything. That is called *dakṣa.*

\* \* \*

Impersonalists see the world as illusion; therefore, they attempt to renounce all worldly interactions. A devotee’s vision, however, is different. A devotee sees the world as Kṛṣṇa’s. Therefore he renounces not his own activities (which are performed in Kṛṣṇa’s service), but the attachment to enjoying the fruit of those activities. Devotees preach on behalf of Kṛṣṇa, and they are often called upon to deal with the world in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Our success, as Prabhupāda pointed out, will be to both accomplish our mission and maintain our devotion. Accomplishing one and not the other is insufficient.

Prabhupāda wanted his disciples to graft his desire that Kṛṣṇa consciousness be spread throughout the world to their own hearts. Without his “big” desire becoming ours, our own petty desires will triumph.

## Surrender to the Desire for Service

**Prabhupāda:** It is not possible to give up desires. That is not possible. To give up desires means I am dead body, a stone. If I have got life, if I am not a stone, there must be desires.  
If we make a desire, it must be fulfilled. Kṛṣṇa will give you facilities. Desire is so strong.  
We must change the platform to no material desires by simply desiring how to serve Kṛṣṇa. *Nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ* means those who are always ready to abide by the orders of Nārāyaṇa.  
Those who have taken the path of Nārāyaṇa, devotional service, as their life and soul, are called *nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ.* So what is the qualification of *nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ*? It does not require any qualification to become *nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ* other than voluntarily agreeing that my life is now dedicated for Nārāyaṇa. If you simply take it as vow that “From this day my life is dedicated to Nārāyaṇa, Kṛṣṇa,” … As soon as we take this vow, that “From this day my life is dedicated to Kṛṣṇa. I surrender,” from that day you become free. Simply this determination that “From this day I belong to Nārāyaṇa, or Kṛṣṇa. Whatever He wants …”

\* \* \*

Desire continues as long as life remains within the body. Accepting Kṛṣṇa’s desire as our desire is spiritual life. Prabhupāda was confident that all factionalism would disappear if the devotees fully surrendered and adopted Kṛṣṇa’s desire as their own.

## Come to the Side of Kṛṣṇa

**Prabhupāda:** That is the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā.* Just to teach us, Arjuna thought as if he was for his family. In the warfield, when there was to be fighting, he came to the family side and he said, “My dear Kṛṣṇa, how can I fight with my family? They are my brothers. They are my nephews. They are my grandfathers. They are my teachers. Fight means to fight with enemies. What kind of war it is that I have to fight with my family?”  
Because he belonged to the Kuru’s family, he took the side of the family. But after hearing *Bhagavad-gītā,* after understanding Kṛṣṇa, when he came to the conclusion that “I must be on Kṛṣṇa’s side, not the family side,” then he became perfect. After speaking the *Bhagavad-gītā,* Kṛṣṇa said, “Now what you will do? To the family side or My side?” Freedom is there. “Whatever you like, you can do. I have given My instruction.” That freedom is there always. Kṛṣṇa or His representative, the spiritual master, he can give you good instruction, good advice. But to do it or not to do it is your option.  
That is your option. You decide yourself. Kṛṣṇa said, *yathecchasi tathā kuru:* “I am not interfering with your independence. I am dealing as your spiritual master, and you accepted Me as your spiritual master.” Arjuna had already accepted earlier by saying, *śiṣyas te ‘haṁ:* “Kṛṣṇa, no more friendly talk. I accept You as my spiritual master.” What kind of disciple? *Prapannam,* fully surrendered. “Now whatever You will say, I’ll do.” This is *śiṣya.*  
I have several times explained, *śiṣya: śas-dhātu*—he who voluntarily accepts the ruling of a person. He is *śiṣya.* And otherwise, “You go on talking whatever nonsense you can. I’ll do my own business.” That is not *śiṣya. Śiṣya* means *śiṣyas te ‘haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam. Tad viddhi praṇipātena.* That is *śiṣya.* So Kṛṣṇa taught him *Bhagavad-gītā.* Then, still, He gave him the freedom, *yathecchasi tathā kuru:* “Now Arjuna, you decide what to do.” It is not force.  
Arjuna’s reply was, “My illusion is now over. I am now remembering what I am.” That remembering means, “I am not belonging to this family.” *Tvat-prasādān … mayācyuta. Smṛtir labdhā.* In the material world we have lost our memory that “I am part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa and my duty is to serve Kṛṣṇa.”

\* \* \*

*Sarvopādhi-vinirmuktaṁ tat-paratvena nirmalam* is the *Nārada Pancarātra’s* definition of pure devotional service. A pure devotee acts as Kṛṣṇa’s servant by serving his spiritual master’s instructions and acting for his pleasure.

## Further Surgery

I was not present during the conversation between Prabhupāda, Siddha-svarūpa, and the other devotees, nor did I find out about it until years later. Still, it proved important for me because based on that conversation, Prabhupāda called me into his presence.

“Prabhupāda wants to see you,” Hari Śauri told me one day. Knowing Kṛṣṇa had a plan, I raced to the balcony of the Honolulu temple where Prabhupāda waited, sitting in a chair. Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa and Hari Śauri sat at his feet. I offered my obeisances.

**Bhūrijana:** Would you like me to do anything? What would you like me to do? I know I don’t want to be independent.  
**Prabhupāda:** Why you are inclined to follow Siddha-svarūpa? What is your special attraction?  
**Bhūrijana:** While we were in Hong Kong, we couldn’t always be corresponding with you. I needed some personal instruction.  
**Prabhupāda:** But what is the special attraction? Your wife said that their instruction is very clear. So what is the distinction between clear and ambiguous? What do you find ambiguous, what do you find clear? What is that?  
**Bhūrijana:** I think the part I found clear, the more introspective points about humility, and changing one’s desires …  
**Prabhupāda:** Humility means not to follow the instruction of *guru*?  
**Bhūrijana:** No, that is not humility.  
**Prabhupāda:** I’m asking you what is the special attraction? What is clear and ambiguous?  
**Bhūrijana:** The clearness may be a deeper understanding to want to be humble. A deeper understanding to want to …  
**Prabhupāda:** Humble, but if you do not follow your spiritual master’s instruction, you follow others, then where is the humbleness? Your wife says that what Siddha-svarūpa says it is very clear and others are not so clear. Is it not? What is that clear, what is not clear?  
**Bhūrijana:** He says chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.  
**Prabhupāda:** So who says that you don’t chant?  
**Bhūrijana:** Maybe the emphasis of others isn’t so much on chanting. Everyone says *harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam,* but everyone only chants their sixteen rounds a day, chants a half-hour *ārati* in the evening and half-hour *ārati* in the morning.  
**Prabhupāda:** So what do you want more? What is your program? Twenty-four hours?  
**Bhūrijana:** No, but I like more *kīrtana* than that.

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda’s probing forced me to consider my relationship with Siddha-svarūpa. He seemed intent on penetrating my consciousness with his questions. He asked what I found different, clearer, or especially attractive that would explain my inclination to specifically follow Siddha-svarūpa and to not follow the regular ISKCON program. He would not accept the difference as “more humility” because my leaving Hong Kong had not demonstrated to him a humble disciple’s submission to his *guru’s* order. My reply that the difference was more emphasis on chanting the holy names caused him to offer a sarcastic reply.

The specific memory I have of this conversation with Prabhupāda was that he was pushing me to reveal my mind. I clearly remember how difficult it was to clarify my thoughts and speak openly and honestly. Prabhupāda was such an awe-inspiring figure. His presence evoked the desire to please him. I feared expressing my possibly wrong or foolish thoughts to his scrutiny, and my tendency was to superficially agree. But Prabhupāda was determined to push through to a deeper, more trusting affection. He forced me to pull out and reveal whatever doubts and complaints I truly harbored. As the conversation continued, I found myself becoming more relaxed and open.

Although I thought that I had both increased my humility and my desire to chant Kṛṣṇa’s names, Prabhupāda wished to point out what he had concluded as the actual difference between ISKCON and the followers of Siddha-svarūpa. It seems that he had already decided what the difference was and he wished me to accept it as well.

**Prabhupāda:** So you don’t like to sell books.  
**Bhūrijana:** No, I think I like very much. I mean I think I would like to.  
**Prabhupāda:** So what is the difference?  
**Bhūrijana:** Between selling books and *kīrtana*?  
**Prabhupāda:** No, no, between others and you. Where is the difference? Point out that this is the point on which we differ. What is that point?  
**Bhūrijana:** Well, maybe the point about more *kīrtana* or less *kīrtana.*  
**Prabhupāda:** So if you chant more, they’re objecting?  
**Bhūrijana:** No.  
**Prabhupāda:** Then where is the difference? Has anyone objected if you chant more?  
**Bhūrijana:** No.  
**Prabhupāda:** Then where is the objection? What is the point of difference?  
**Bhūrijana:** I think there’s no point of difference.  
**Prabhupāda:** Then why do you say we find these instructions more clear?  
**Bhūrijana:** It’s just the emphasis that was given to chanting. For myself, I know that in my heart I built up an enmity toward your disciples and thinking that by their distributing books, they were making people angry at Kṛṣṇa and …  
**Prabhupāda:** That is the real point. That is the real point.

\* \* \*

I was in Japan and Hong Kong from 1970–1974 and then lived outside mainstream ISKCON from 1974–1976. During these foundational years of ISKCON, Prabhupāda had made known his desires about book distribution. Even as I sat at Prabhupāda’s feet on the balcony outside his rooms in ISKCON Hawaii, I was unaware of the depth of Prabhupāda’s desire that his disciples distribute his books. Yet by his pushing me to analyze the actual difference, it eventually popped out: the difference between how the inside and the outside factions were prosecuting Kṛṣṇa consciousness was the emphasis on book distribution, or deeper than that, the depth of surrender to Prabhupāda’s order to distribute books.

**Bhūrijana:** That’s what I felt. When I was in Hong Kong, people I would meet, they used to yell … they’d yell at me, “What have you done to Kṛṣṇa?” Some Indians used to say that. People we’d meet, they’d tell us they see the Hare Kṛṣṇa devotees and they hate Kṛṣṇa. I remember one specific time when I was speaking to one businessman who was helping us and …  
**Prabhupāda:** He said that, “We hate Kṛṣṇa?”  
**Bhūrijana:** No, no. He said that “Your members of the Hare Kṛṣṇa, they’re making people in Australia hate Kṛṣṇa. They make people inimical.” Making people inimical to Kṛṣṇa.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** They feel threatened simply that we’re preaching something that will discourage their sinful way of life. Simply they feel threatened. Not they’re hating. Their argument is that because of our strong emphasis on preaching and book distribution, they say therefore that the mass of people have a bad impression of Kṛṣṇa.  
**Bhūrijana:** And also because …  
**Prabhupāda:** Because we are selling books.  
**Bhūrijana:** Not so much the selling of books. Maybe because of the emphasis on how much money can be taken. Like if someone says on the street, “Please give me a donation,” so they give him a donation. “No, you must give more,” and more and more. So the people think that the devotees are only interested in getting money and they get a bad impression that ISKCON is a money-making movement.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** We’re selling one-half million large-sized books each year.  
**Prabhupāda:** That is envious. So if they sell books, so that is making Kṛṣṇa unpopular?

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda is agreeing with Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa’s point that books are being distributed, and it is not only that money is being collected.

**Bhūrijana:** But one must learn to be a good book salesman, I think.  
**Prabhupāda:** But selling book about Kṛṣṇa, does it mean that the booksellers are creating unpopular opinion? Does it mean?  
**Bhūrijana:** Automatically, no.  
**Prabhupāda:** When you say that they are making enemies because they are pushing this, what is wrong there? Actually, I can so far understand that you do not like to sell books, or you cannot sell books.  
**Bhūrijana:** I’ve never really tried so much.  
**Prabhupāda:** Those who are selling books, you think of them that they’re not very advanced.  
**Bhūrijana:** I don’t think that they can continue for a very long time if they are not advanced.  
**Prabhupāda:** But actually they’re doing, so why do you say they cannot continue long?

\* \* \*

Prabhupāda points out that book distributors who are engaged year after year in fulfilling his desire are surrendered and advanced devotees.

**Bhūrijana:** It used to make me angry when people would collect money and lie and say that they’re collecting money for the welfare center here, or to feed the children here, or to bus the children there. They’d collect money, but it wasn’t straightforward.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** Prabhupāda has many times given the instruction to the devotees that they should try as much as possible to distribute the books on their own merit.  
**Bhūrijana:** But they emphasize collecting, not distributing books.  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** No, but the point is that Prabhupāda’s instruction is there.  
**Bhūrijana:** But all the devotees want to hear is Prabhupāda also said, “By hook or by crook.”  
**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** Sometimes it may be required to interest someone in a book that they may find out their interest. Just like people are interested in philanthropic activity.  
**Prabhupāda:** Just like our … What is his name?  
**Hari-śauri:** Tripurāri? \[*Laughter.*\]  
**Prabhupāda:** Some lady inquired, “Is there any instruction about the power shortage?” “Oh, yes.” \[*Laughs.*\] So she purchased, and the next day she said, “There is nothing about power.” So suppose Tripurāri has sold one book. The lady inquired, “Is there any basic instruction about power shortage?” And he said, “Yes.” So you think it is wrong. That is your version.  
**Bhūrijana:** I think a simple lie like that is not bad.  
**Prabhupāda:** But a devotee’s view is that whatever may be her inquiry, if she purchases one book, she’ll be benefited. This is the point. But you, as big moralist, you, \[*Laughs.*\] you think that he has cheated. He has not cheated. He has benefited her. Somehow or other he has given a book to her. Someday she’ll read and she’ll benefit. But you people, you think that why we should sell like that, “Yes, there is some solution of power shortage.” Actually there is, but you think that he has lied. Just like sometimes the father’s child is suffering from fever, so the father wants that the child may take the medicine and promises, “You take the medicine. I’ll give you this cake.” The purpose is not to give the cake actually. The purpose is the child may take the medicine. That is the purpose. But when the father promises, “If you take the medicine, I’ll give you the cake.” But cake is dangerous for the child who is suffering from fever, but still the father allures him. “If you take the medicine and I’ll give you cake.” So is the father wrong?  
**Bhūrijana:** No.  
**Prabhupāda:** Similarly, if somehow or other one can sell one book to a person, it is good for him. Don’t take how he is selling, but he’s giving the book and that person is paying something. That is good.
